The [Gaza war](74998519.md) has sparked protests, demonstrations, and vigils around the world.[^cite_note-apphotos11october-19] These protests focused on a variety of issues related to the conflict, including demands for a [ceasefire](592591.md), an end to the [Israeli blockade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2023_Israeli_blockade_of_the_Gaza_Strip) and [occupation](2781576.md), return of Israeli [hostages](75025451.md), protesting [war crimes](75113830.md), ending [US support for Israel](65284610.md) and providing [humanitarian aid](434679.md) to [Gaza](12047.md). Since the war began on 7 October 2023, the death toll has exceeded 70,000.[^cite_note-20]

Some of the protests have resulted in violence and accusations of [antisemitism](1078.md) and [anti-Palestinianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Palestinian_racism_during_the_Gaza_war).[^cite_note-\:12-21][^cite_note-\:13-22][^cite_note-23] In some European countries, and Palestine itself, protestors were criminalized, in countries such as [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md), and [Hungary](13275.md), which restricted [pro-Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Palestinian) political speech, while the Israeli media outlet *[Ynet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ynet)* reported that [Hamas](13913.md) in Gaza tortured and executed [anti-Hamas demonstrators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_Strip_anti-Hamas_protests).[^cite_note-ineurf-24][^cite_note-HamasCrackdown-25] The conflict also sparked large protests at [Israeli](6838552.md) and [U.S. embassies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._embassies) around the world.[^cite_note-26]

Nearly 48,000 demonstrations[^cite_note-27] have been held globally,[^cite_note-28] making up 15% of all demonstrations worldwide. Only approximately 1% of all Gaza war protests have turned violent.[^cite_note-29]

1. [Middle East](19323.md) 22,066 (46.0%): 22,066 events

2. [Europe](9239.md) & [Central Asia](6742.md) 8,298 (17.3%): 8,298 events

3. [Africa](5334607.md) 6,528 (13.6%): 6,528 events

4. [United States](3434750.md) & [Canada](5042916.md) 6,097 (12.7%): 6,097 events

5. [Asia-Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific) 4,556 (9.50%): 4,556 events

6. [Latin America](18524.md) & [the Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Caribbean) 436 (0.91%): 436 events

## Israel

Main article: [Gaza war protests in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel)

[Israeli citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_citizenship_law) participated in public protests related to war policies.[^cite_note-antinet-7] Jewish Israeli anti-war activists experienced targeting by far-right groups,[^cite_note-Inside_the_Israeli_Crackdown-30] while [Palestinian citizens of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_citizens_of_Israel) experienced crackdowns on free speech, with people arrested for social media posts and likes.[^cite_note-Inside_the_Israeli_Crackdown-30] Anti-war protests erupted in the [Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triangle_\(Israel\)) and [Galilee](12639.md), with Israeli police repressing demonstrators in [Umm El Fahm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umm_El_Fahm), a city in the Triangle.[^cite_note-31] In March, another protest occurred in the city, urging Israel to "end its criminal war"[^cite_note-32]

A hostage-solidarity rally in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) on 14 October criticized the government's handling of the war and called on Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) to resign.[^cite_note-bizin-8][^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] The next day, communications minister [Shlomo Karhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shlomo_Karhi) proposed emergency regulations allowing for the arrest of people who hurt "national morale".[^cite_note-35] Following a rally in support of Gaza in [Haifa](55606.md), police commissioner [Kobi Shabtai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobi_Shabtai) threatened to send antiwar protesters to the Gaza Strip on buses.[^cite_note-36] On 28 October, a mob of Israelis gathered at [Netanya Academic College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netanya_Academic_College) chanting "[death to Arabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_Arabs)" at Palestinian students living in the campus dormitories.[^cite_note-\:13-22][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38]

On 4 November, protests were held near Netanyahu's residence.[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] On 8 November, the [Israel Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Supreme_Court) allowed police to bar all anti-war protests.[^cite_note-41] On 9 November, Israeli police arrested former member of the [Knesset](74656.md) [Mohammad Barakeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Barakeh) in [Nazareth](21702.md) for attempting to organize an anti-war protest.[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-43] In an interview with *[Time Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Magazine)*, Barakeh described Israel's crackdowns on free speech as [fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist).[^cite_note-44] On 18 November, Israel held its first permitted anti-war protest in [Tel Aviv](31453.md).[^cite_note-45]

The [Knesset](74656.md) criminalized the "consumption of terrorist materials" on 8 November.[^cite_note-46] Civil rights groups criticized the bill, stating it "invades the realm of personal thoughts and beliefs".[^cite_note-47] On 2 December, an attorney at [Adalah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adalah_\(legal_center\)), an Israeli legal center, stated law enforcement was using the law to surveil and silence people, while [Association for Civil Rights in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_for_Civil_Rights_in_Israel) said it was "unprecedented in democratic countries".[^cite_note-48]

On 20 November, [Kan 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kan_11) posted and then deleted a video produced by a private NGO, showing children who were evacuated from [Gaza envelope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_envelope) settlements singing "we'll eliminate them all and go back to plowing our fields".[^cite_note-49] On 23 November, communications minister Shlomo Karhi proposed defunding the newspaper *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* due to its "defeatist and false propaganda".[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] On 23 November, ahead of the [hostage-swap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire), National Security Minister [Itamar Ben Gvir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itamar_Ben_Gvir) stated that any "expressions of joy" related to the release of Palestinian prisoners was "equivalent to backing terrorism".[^cite_note-52]

On 25 November, protesters in [Jerusalem](16043.md) called on Netanyahu to resign.[^cite_note-53] Jews and Israelis abroad participated in protests both supporting and protesting the Israeli response in the war.[^cite_note-Tamkin-54][^cite_note-painfully-55] On 29 November, police arrested activists at a Knesset protest opposing the government.[^cite_note-56] Six were arrested on 2 December protesting outside Netanyahu's house in [Caesarea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesarea_\(modern_town\)).[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58] A suspected arson-originated fire was reported at a protest camp for the families of hostages held in Gaza; the families experienced harassment from right-wing Netanyahu supporters.[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60]

On 16 December, protesters set up tents outside the [Israeli Defense Ministry](3226446.md) in Tel Aviv and stated they would not leave until the government took up hostage negotiations again.[^cite_note-61] Two activists in [Haifa](55606.md) were arrested for quietly protesting against the war.[^cite_note-62]



Anti-war protest in Tel Aviv, Israel, 22 July 2025

On 26 December 2023, Israeli teenager Tal Mitnick was sentenced to 30 days in jail for refusing the draft for the war which he condemned as "a revenge campaign... not only against Hamas, but against all Palestinian people". This comment was shared by one of several support groups for [conscientious objectors](83003.md) to the IDF.[^cite_note-63]

In August 2025, a nationwide protest billed as a "day of struggle" was seen across Israel with protestors calling for a hostage and ceasefire deal. The largest protest was seen in Tel Aviv with more than 300,000 people attending the protest according to organizers, with many expressing doubt in Netanyahu's government.[^cite_note-64]

### Open letters

Some groups in Israel expressed support for the war. Dozens of [rabbis](51273.md) signed a letter to Netanyahu and senior defence officials stating that "even when the enemy hides behind a [human shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield)... there is no [halakhic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halakha) or moral preclusion, nor legal preclusion, from bombing the enemy after sufficient advance warning".[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66]

A group of 100 doctors called "Doctors for the Rights of Israeli Soldiers", signed a statement stating Israel had a "legitimate right" to bomb "terror nests and [Hamas headquarters in the hospitals in Gaza](74998519.md#Hamas_facilities)".[^cite_note-67] This prompted a harsh reply from the acting head of the ethics office at the [Israel Medical Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Medical_Association), Dr. Tami Karni, who wrote that "doctors are sworn to heal, not kill... Israel's doctors have refused to be dragged into the consciental and moral decline of the enemy, and will continue to act accordingly". The White Robes, an organization which numbers in the hundreds, decried the letter as a "provocative pamphlet by an extremist minority", stating that "calls for indiscriminate destruction and killing, even if justified militarily, are not part of the medical ethical code". [Physicians for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights) published an open letter signed by 350 physicians and medical personnel, which also condemns the aforementioned call.[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69]

In September 2023, over 200 Israeli youth released an open letter entitled [Youth Against Dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_Against_Dictatorship), announcing that they would refuse to serve.[^cite_note-70]

## Palestine

### West Bank and East Jerusalem

On 12 October 2023, [Hamas](13913.md) called for Palestinians to protest in [East Jerusalem](343508.md) and the [West Bank](33209.md), encouraging demonstrations at the [Al-Aqsa Mosque](65759.md).[^cite_note-71] They also called for protests in neighboring countries and around the world, urging Muslim communities to rally in support of Gaza.[^cite_note-snopes-72][^cite_note-checknews-73]

After the [Al-Ahli Arab Hospital explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion), protests broke out in the West Bank in support of Gaza.[^cite_note-74] On 27 October, hundreds rallied in [Ramallah](44132.md) to support Gaza, despite fears of [settler violence](47246246.md).[^cite_note-75] On 1 November 2023, a [general strike](41138909.md) was observed in the West Bank and [East Jerusalem](343508.md) in opposition to the Israeli attacks on Gaza.[^cite_note-76] On 5 November, protesters in Ramallah protested US Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken)'s visit to the West Bank, holding signs reading "Blinken, blood is on your hands".[^cite_note-77] On 17 November 2023, Israeli forces fired [tear gas](1202646.md) on anti-war protestors in [Hebron](38577.md).[^cite_note-78]

On 11 December 2023, Palestinians in the West Bank and East Jerusalem went on a general strike as part of a larger global strike for a ceasefire;[^cite_note-79] the action also led to the closure of establishments, educational institutions, and administrative buildings in the occupied West Bank and East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-80] Palestinian activists and grassroots organizations urged for a worldwide strike. The call for action, which gained momentum through social media, aims to encompass "all facets of public existence" and was considered on 11 December.[^cite_note-81]

On 23 December 2023, the Christmas nativity scene in [Bethlehem](4312.md) honored the deceased in Gaza.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] Boy and Girl Scouts in the West Bank displayed a banner commemorating the child victims of the war during Christmas Eve celebrations.[^cite_note-84] Protesters in Ramallah carried a banner with the names of thousands of the deceased in Gaza on 1 January 2024.[^cite_note-85] On 3 January 2024, Palestinians held a general protest in the West Bank.[^cite_note-86] On 10 January, Palestinians gathered at Nelson Mandela Square in Ramallah to express support for *[South Africa v. Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel_\(Genocide_Convention\))*, a lawsuit charging Israel with [genocide in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza) at the [International Court of Justice](14918.md).[^cite_note-87] On 7 February, protesters demonstrated at UN headquarters in Ramallah against the suspension of aid.[^cite_note-88]

On 9 February 2024, Palestinians and Israelis protesting in support of a ceasefire were reportedly attacked by police in the West Bank.[^cite_note-89] On 10 February, Israeli police broke up a Gaza peace protest in [Jericho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jericho).[^cite_note-90] Paramedics in the West Bank protested against the Israeli killing of two [Palestinian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Red_Crescent) emergency respondents killed while attempting to rescue [Hind Rajab](76056228.md).[^cite_note-91] Protesters demonstrated in Ramallah on 17 February 2024.[^cite_note-92] In March 2024, the city of [Jericho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jericho) unveiled a street named after [Aaron Bushnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Bushnell), a U.S. servicemen who self-immolated in protest of U.S. support for Israel.[^cite_note-93] Large protests were held in [Arraba, Jenin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arraba,_Jenin) in protest of the Israeli killing of a man named Muhammad Jaber.[^cite_note-94] On 21 March 2024, residents in Jenin went on strike in protest of the killing of three young men.[^cite_note-95] On 26 March 2024, video showed hundreds of people in [Nur Shams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nur_Shams) refugee camp protesting against Israeli actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-96] In August 2024, a report by [7amleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7amleh) found that young Palestinians in the West Bank and East Jerusalem were self-censoring online due to fears of repercussions.[^cite_note-97]

### Gaza Strip



Palestinian protesters in the Gaza Strip, 16 October 2023. Photo by [Anas Al-Sharif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anas_Al-Sharif).

In Gaza, young [content creators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_creation), such as [Hind Khoudary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hind_Khoudary), [Plestia Alaqad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plestia_Alaqad), [Motaz Azaiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motaz_Azaiza), and [Bisan Owda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisan_Owda), documented their lives through the war, gaining significant followings on social media.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100]

In January 2024, the *[Jewish News Syndicate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_News_Syndicate)* reported that in a rare protest against Hamas, dozens of Gazan children held up signs outside [Deir al-Balah](1470463.md)'s [al-Aqsa Martyrs Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa_Martyrs_Hospital), asking Hamas to free Israeli hostages and end the war, expressing their desire to return home. The IDF's [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic) spokesman [Avichay Adraee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avichay_Adraee) shared a recording of the protest on [X](9988187.md), tweeting "Will these cries and demands reach the hideouts of Hamas leaders?". The protest occurred a day after another small protest against Hamas in [Rafah](584373.md) where Palestinians cursed Hamas and Hamas leader [Yahya Sinwar](53195047.md). Organized demonstration against Hamas is not allowed in Gaza.[^cite_note-101] In February 2024, [Sveriges Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sveriges_Radio) reported on some small spontaneous protests by desperate Gazans against Hamas.[^cite_note-102]

A small group of children in [Rafah](584373.md) held their own protest in advance of a planned [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) in February 2024, holding signs in English that read "We refuse to die" and "Save us from this genocide".[^cite_note-103] Protesters at [UNRWA](32270.md) headquarters in [Jabalia](18169472.md) called for more food, chanting, "We want flour, we want flour".[^cite_note-104] Children in Rafah again held their own protest against the [Gaza Strip famine](75828824.md) on 6 March, holding a banner reading "Stop our daily death".[^cite_note-105]

On 25 March 2025, [large anti-Hamas and anti-war protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gazan_anti-Hamas_protests) broke out in the [North Gaza Governorate](8689663.md).[^cite_note-106] Israeli media outlet *[Ynet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ynet)* reported that Hamas responded by torturing and executing some of those who organized the protests.[^cite_note-HamasCrackdown-25]

On 26 June 2026, [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_26_Revolution) were held in Gaza City and other regions of northern Gaza against Hamas.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108]

## Africa

### Algeria

Protests were held in [Algiers](1644.md) on 19 October 2023 denouncing [U.S. President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_President) [Joe Biden](145422.md), Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and Arab countries that have [normalized relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_normalization) with Israel for "complicity" in the violence in Gaza.[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110] On 4 April 2025, at least 800 pro-Palestine protestors gathered in front of the headquarters of the [Movement of Society for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movement_of_Society_for_Peace) in Algiers.[^cite_note-111]

### Botswana

In October and November 2023, marches were held in the capital, [Gaborone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaborone) to show solidarity with the Palestinians.[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113]

### Djibouti

Hundreds of people, including foreign nationals, participated in a pro-Palestine march in [Arta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arta,_Djibouti) in November 2023.[^cite_note-114]

### Ghana

A National March for Palestine was held in [Accra](56824.md) on 2 November, drawing thousands of participants.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117]

### Kenya

On 21 October 2023, hundreds of demonstrators in [Mombasa](200152.md) showcased banners and waved the Palestinian flag, while slogans like "Cease the Violence", "End the Suffering of Innocent Lives" and "United with Gaza" were displayed.[^cite_note-118] A protest in solidarity with Palestine was later held in [Nairobi](21482.md) on 25 January 2024 but was dispersed by police using tear gas.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] A pro-Palestine protest was also held on 24 February in Mombasa.[^cite_note-121]

### Libya

In May 2024, students, faculty members, and employees at the [University of Tripoli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tripoli) organized a demonstration in support of Palestine.[^cite_note-122] In January 2025, dozens of Libyans in [Tripoli](31232.md), [Misrata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misrata) and other cities organized protests when an interview by the [Government of National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Unity_\(Libya\))'s former Foreign Minister, [Najla El Mangoush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najla_El_Mangoush) was broadcast on Al Jazeera, in which she said the government of Prime Minister [Abdul Hamid Dbeibeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Hamid_Dbeibeh) had coordinated her secret meeting with Israeli Foreign Minister [Eli Cohen](46195208.md) in Rome in August 2023.[^cite_note-123]

### Malawi

In November 2023, hundreds of people in [Blantyre](642604.md) protested against Israel's war on Gaza.[^cite_note-124]

### Mauritania

In October 2023, hundreds of people joined a pro-Palestine rally and marched through [Nouakchott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouakchott).[^cite_note-125]

### Morocco

On 19 November, protests were held in [Tangier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangier) in support of Palestine, demanding an end to the [normalization of ties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Morocco_relations) between Morocco and Israel.[^cite_note-\:4-126] A similar protest was held in [Casablanca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casablanca) on 26 November.[^cite_note-127] Protesters in February 2024 called for the end of normalization with Israel, holding banners that read, "Normalisation is treason".[^cite_note-128] In [Rabat](57400.md), thousands marched in solidarity with the Palestinian people.[^cite_note-GalleryAlJazeera20231015-129] On 28 June, protests were held in Tangier after Morocco granted an Israeli warship entry to its port.[^cite_note-130] Demonstrations were also held in [Fez](39760328.md), [Meknes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meknes), [Kenitra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenitra), [Agadir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agadir), [Berkane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkane), [Oujda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oujda) and [Jerada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerada).[^cite_note-aje.io-131] Protesters held banners and chanted slogans in support of Palestinians.[^cite_note-apnews.com-132] On 6 October 2024, a large demonstration broke out in Rabat with people calling for the end of normalization with Israel.[^cite_note-133] On 20 April 2025, nearly 1,000 people in Tangier protested against a planned docking of a *[Maersk](723005.md)* cargo ship that was carrying parts of [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II](11812.md) aircraft to Israel.[^cite_note-134]

### Nigeria

Around 50,000 protesters held a rally expressing solidarity with Palestine organized by the Conference of Islamic Organisations at [Gani Fawehinmi Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gani_Fawehinmi_Park), [Lagos](85232.md) on 21 October. They demanded the Nigerian government halt the diplomatic relations with Israel until a two-state solution was reached.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] Hundreds of members of the [Islamic Movement of Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Movement_of_Nigeria) protested on the streets of [Kaduna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaduna_\(city\)) against Israel's invasion of Gaza on 16 November. The protest resulted in clashes with police that left one person dead and several others injured. Police and the IMN both blamed each other for the death.[^cite_note-africanews.com-12] Another INM protest in [Abuja](56823.md) on 28 March 2025 resulted in clashes with police that left five INM members and one police officer dead.[^cite_note-ShibayanAP20250329-11]

### Senegal

On 4 November 2023, 200 protesters gathered outside the [Grand Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Mosque_of_Dakar) in [Dakar](44251.md) carrying the Palestinian flag and placards denouncing genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-137] On 4 May 2024, pro-Palestinian marches and demonstrations were held in Dakar.[^cite_note-138] On 26 May, hundreds protested at the Palestinian embassy in Dakar.[^cite_note-139]

On 13 April 2025, hundreds of people gathered at a pro-Palestine demonstration in Dakar to protest against Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-140] On 27 May, Israel's ambassador to Senegal, Yuval Waks, was forced to leave [Cheikh Anta Diop University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheikh_Anta_Diop_University) after students protested his presence for a conference on international relations.[^cite_note-141]

### Seychelles

On 27 January 2024, members of the 'Seychelles for Palestine' organised a peaceful march in [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria,_Seychelles), calling for a ceasefire in Gaza.[^cite_note-142]

### Somalia

On 20 October 2023, Somalis took to the streets in [Mogadishu](60154.md) to protest the Israeli attacks on the Gaza Strip and to show solidarity with Palestinians, shouting slogans such as "Stop the war crime", "Free Free Palestine", and "We demand an end to the genocide in Gaza, Palestine."[^cite_note-143]

On 25 July 2025, as a part of the *Gaza day of action* protests erupted in Mogadishu with protestors chanting against [Israel's starvation of Gaza](75828824.md).[^cite_note-144]

### South Africa

Hundreds marched in [Cape Town](6653.md) on 13 October to protest against Israeli attacks on the Gaza Strip. In an address to the crowd. [Mandla Mandela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandla_Mandela), a grandson of [Nelson Mandela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Mandela), called on President [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md) to speak on the Gaza war at the [UN General Assembly](31957.md) and act on the crisis.[^cite_note-145]

On 11 November, thousands marched in Cape Town demanding the expulsion of the Israeli ambassador and the closure of the Israeli embassy.[^cite_note-146] On 11 January 2024, a statue of [Desmond Tutu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desmond_Tutu) wearing a [keffiyeh](47446641.md) was erected in [Cape Town](6653.md).[^cite_note-147] On 13 January, protesters marched to the US consulate in [Johannesburg](58893.md) to demand a ceasefire.[^cite_note-148]

### Tanzania

In July 2024, several activists and influencers in [Zanzibar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zanzibar) showed solidarity with Palestinians.[^cite_note-149]

### Tunisia

Thousands gathered outside the French embassy in [Tunis](57659.md) to protest Western support for Israel, chanting that "the French and the Americans are partners in the attack" against Palestinians. Some expressed support for Hamas by shouting,wp "Dear (Ezzedine) al-Qassam (Brigades), destroy [Tel Aviv](31453.md)", in reference to the movement's military wing. A similar protest occurred outside the US embassy in Tunis' northern suburbs.[^cite_note-\:3-150] An estimated 3,000 people participated in the demonstrations.[^cite_note-151] On 31 July 2024, a crowd marched in [Sfax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sfax) in support of Palestine,[^cite_note-152] Another protest was held in Tunis after the [assassination](77478235.md) of Hamas leader [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md).[^cite_note-GalleryAlJazeera20240801-153]

## Asia

### Afghanistan

On 13 October, demonstrators gathered at the [Eidgah Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eidgah_Mosque) in [Kabul](16826.md) to express support for Palestine.[^cite_note-154]

### Azerbaijan

In November 2024, dozens of people gathered at the [2024 United Nations Climate Change Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference) in [Baku](4566.md) to express solidarity with Palestine and call for a ceasefire in Gaza.[^cite_note-155]

### Bangladesh

Further information: [March for Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Gaza)



Pro-Palestine protest in [Kishoreganj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kishoreganj_District), Bangladesh

Activists from [Islami Andolan Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islami_Andolan_Bangladesh) staged protests against Israel's military actions in Gaza and expressed their solidarity with the Palestinian people in front of the [Baitul Mukarram National Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baitul_Mukarram_National_Mosque) in [Dhaka](56656.md).[^cite_note-apphotos11october-19] The protesters held banners reading "Free Palestine" and "Stop Genocide".[^cite_note-156] On 9 May 2024, protests were held in Dhaka demanding the end of the "genocide in Palestine".[^cite_note-157]

On 7 April 2025, nationwide protests were held in solidarity with the "Global Strike for Gaza" campaign. Many educational institutions voluntarily closed to support the global "No Work, No School" campaign.[^cite_note-158] Protests were also held in front of the US Embassy in Dhaka, after which the embassy issued a "protest alert" for Americans residing in Bangladesh.[^cite_note-159]

On 12 April, Bangladesh held the largest ever [pro-Palestine protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Gaza) in history, gaining over 1,000,000 participants in [Suhrawardy Udyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suhrawardy_Udyan), Dhaka.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162]

### Brunei

On 6 November 2023, more than 8,000 people gathered for a peace walk in [Taman Mahkota Jubli Emas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taman_Mahkota_Jubli_Emas) to show solidarity with the Palestinians.[^cite_note-163] On 12 November, around 80 people held a march in [Kampong Kapok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kampong_Kapok) to show solidarity with the Palestinians.[^cite_note-164]

### Cambodia

In September 2024 during [Pchum Ben](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pchum_Ben), more than 100 people gathered in [Phnom Penh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phnom_Penh) to express solidary with the people of Palestine.[^cite_note-165]

### China

In Chinese media interviews with students who just finished the 2024 [Gaokao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaokao) (China's national undergraduate admission exam), some students expressed prayers for peace in the Middle East and solidarity with Palestine. Videos posted online showed students displaying the Palestinian flag or stating in interviews that "[from the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea), Palestine will be free." By the last day of the exam, many of the original interview clips had been taken down, but clips that were later reposted by individual netizens were not deleted.[^cite_note-166]

#### Hong Kong

On 4 November 2023, five anti-war activists rallied near [Hong Kong](13404.md)'s Israeli consulate, displaying a list of thousands of Palestinians killed by Israel's bombardment of the Gaza Strip. The activists were from HK Anti-war Mobilization and gathered in [Admiralty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admiralty,_Hong_Kong), holding signs reading "Stand with Palestine," whilst chanting "Israeli army destroyed Gaza." Plainclothes police officers were on the scene, as the activists held a three-minute silence.[^cite_note-167]

On 8 November 2025, three activists protested at Emperor Cinemas in [Times Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Times_Square_\(Hong_Kong\)) to demonstrate against the [Hong Kong Jewish Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Jewish_Film_Festival), calling for a boycott of the "artwashing" of Israel's war in Gaza.[^cite_note-168]

### Georgia

In October 2023, a pro-Israel rally was held at the [Great Synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Synagogue_\(Tbilisi\)) in [Tbilisi](42298.md) to express solidarity with Israel.[^cite_note-169] On 31 October, about a hundred people attended a pro-Palestine rally in [Marneuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marneuli) to protest against Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-170]

### India

On 13 October, pro-Palestinian protests erupted in three cities. In [Hyderabad](37534.md), people chanted slogans like "Long live Palestine" and "Gaza will never die" and police, upon arriving on the scene, broke up the protest and removed demonstrators from the spot. In [Budgam district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budgam_district), [Jammu and Kashmir](61449812.md), protesters chanted slogans against Israel and the United States, one of which was "We stand with Palestine".[^cite_note-\:1-171] To avoid unrest, the main mosque in [Srinagar](294148.md) was closed during [Friday prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friday_prayer).[^cite_note-\:0-172] In [Lucknow](392095.md), a cleric accused Israel of committing war crimes on Palestine and appealed to Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) to intervene in the war and bring a halt to the hostilities in Gaza.[^cite_note-\:1-171] Pro-Palestinian rallies later experienced a crackdown by Indian police while pro-Israeli protests were allowed.[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174]

Several pro-Palestinian demonstrators were also detained while trying to protest in [Delhi](37756.md).[^cite_note-175]

On 26 October, a pro-Palestine rally was held in [Kozhikode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kozhikode), [Kerala](4349459.md), which drew 200,000 people and was organized by the [Indian Union Muslim League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Union_Muslim_League). Another rally on 11 November drew 50,000 participants and was organized by the [Communist Party of India (Marxist)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India_\(Marxist\)) and inaugurated by Chief Minister [Pinarayi Vijayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinarayi_Vijayan), during which he denounced what he called the "Zionist bias" of the Modi government and called on it to sever military agreements and diplomatic relations with Israel.[^cite_note-176]

On 20 August 2025, hundreds of people attended a pro-Palestine protest led by the [Communist Party of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_India_\(Marxist\)) at [Azad Maidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azad_Maidan) in [Mumbai](19189.md), demanding an end of the [genocide in Gaza](75668427.md).[^cite_note-177]

On 19 September, more than 5,000 people joined a pro-Palestine rally in [Pudupet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pudupet_\(Chennai\)), [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai) to protest against Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-178] The event was organised by the Coalition of Periyarists with demonstrators chanted "Free Palestine" and "Free [Tamil Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Eelam)".[^cite_note-179]

### Indonesia

Further information: [2023 Bitung clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Bitung_clashes)

[Abu Bakar Ba'asyir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Bakar_Ba'asyir), one of the suspects of the [2002 Bali bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Bali_bombings), joined protests in [Solo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surakarta). In a speech with protesters holding [Palestinian flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_flag), he said that "we cannot be weak in facing Israel" and that "hopefully, amongst these young people, there are some ready to be sent to (Palestinian territories)".[^cite_note-\:0-172] On 20 October, around 300 members of the Solidarity Committee for Palestine and Yemen held a pro-Palestine protest in front of the [US Embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Jakarta) in [Jakarta](16275.md) and later marched into the UN Office.[^cite_note-180] The Indonesian Coalition to Defend Baitul Maqdis carried a protest in solidarity for Palestine on 28 October in front of the US Embassy.[^cite_note-181] In [Palu](1432484.md), 500 people held a protest expressing support for Palestine in front of a [McDonald's](2480627.md) restaurant on 27 October and demanded to meet the management.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183]

More than two million people participated in the pro-Palestinian rally organized by the Indonesian People's Alliance to Defend Palestine at the [National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Monument_\(Indonesia\)) on 5 November. The rally was attended by officials such as foreign minister [Retno Marsudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retno_Marsudi), religious affairs minister [Yaqut Cholil Qoumas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaqut_Cholil_Qoumas), and education minister [Muhadjir Effendy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhadjir_Effendy), parliament speaker [Puan Maharani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puan_Maharani), former Jakarta governor [Anies Baswedan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anies_Baswedan), former Vice President [Jusuf Kalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jusuf_Kalla), former parliament speaker [Amien Rais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amien_Rais), and former [Muhammadiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammadiyah) chairperson [Din Syamsuddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Din_Syamsuddin).[^cite_note-184]

On 12 November, pro-Palestinian rallies were held in [Bekasi](1915107.md), [Cimahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cimahi), [Pekalongan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekalongan), [Surabaya](212635.md), and [Surakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surakarta).[^cite_note-185] The Muslim Solidarity Front held a pro-Palestine rally in [Bitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitung) on 25 November, resulting in [clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Bitung_clashes) with the pro-Israel organization [Pasukan Manguni Makasiouw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasukan_Manguni_Makasiouw).[^cite_note-186]

On 13 January 2024, thousands of people rallied at the [US embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Jakarta) in Jakarta as a part of the "global day of action".[^cite_note-187] On 3 August 2024, people gathered at the US embassy in Jakarta to express their support for Palestinians after the assassination of Ismail Haniyeh.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-188]

On 6 October 2024, people rallied in Jakarta in solidarity with Palestine and Lebanon during [Israel's incursion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon).[^cite_note-ReferenceB-189]

On 15 June 2025, a pro-Palestine rally was held in [Jakarta](16275.md) to protest against Israel's attacks on Gaza.[^cite_note-190]

On 22 November 2025, Indonesia’s biggest Islamic organisation, [Nahdlatul Ulama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahdlatul_Ulama), called on its chairman [Yahya Cholil Staquf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Cholil_Staquf) to resign for inviting an American scholar known for his staunch support of Israel.[^cite_note-191]

### Japan

On 11 October, a pro-Israel rally was held in the [Shibuya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shibuya) district of Tokyo attended by Israeli diplomats.[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193]

Members of the [Japanese Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Japan) community demonstrated in front of the Israeli Embassy in [Tokyo](30057.md) holding signs and chanting slogans such as "Israel, Terrorists" and "Free Palestine".[^cite_note-\:0-172] There were also demonstrations in [Kyoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyoto) on 19 November.[^cite_note-\:4-126]

On 20 November, around 1,500 protesters demonstrated in Tokyo calling for a ceasefire to "Save Gaza".[^cite_note-194] In December, about 1,200 people gathered in Tokyo to march in support of Israel.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196]

On 13 January 2024, as part of the "global day of action", hundreds gathered in Tokyo to protest against the Israeli war in Gaza and demanded a ceasefire.[^cite_note-197] A single protester in Tokyo has demonstrated alone for three months since November 2023 holding a banner reading "Stop Gaza Genocide".[^cite_note-198]

On 4 February, more than 50 pro-Palestinian protesters marched to call for a ceasefire in front of the [Atomic Bomb Dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_Bomb_Dome) in [Hiroshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroshima).[^cite_note-199]

On 11 May, hundreds of demonstrators marched at a pro-Palestine rally in Shibuya in Tokyo. Some chanted "[From the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea), Palestine will be free."[^cite_note-200]

On 15 July, dozens of people participated in a march in solidarity with Gaza in [Namba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namba), [Osaka Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osaka_Prefecture).[^cite_note-201]

On 31 July, [Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagasaki) mayor [Shiro Suzuki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiro_Suzuki_\(politician\)) disinvited representatives from Israel from the upcoming 79th annual commemoration of the US atomic bombing on the city.[^cite_note-202] Ambassadors to Japan [Rahm Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahm_Emanuel) and [Julia Longbottom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Longbottom) from the U.S. and U.K. respectively, refused to attend the Nagasaki ceremony in counter-protest.[^cite_note-203]

On 5 August, a large protest occurred in [Hiroshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroshima) accusing Prime Minister [Fumio Kishida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fumio_Kishida) of being complicit with the genocide in Gaza. On 6 August, the anniversary of the [Atomic bombing of Hiroshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_bombings_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki), the city government [banned gatherings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_assembly) in Peace Park and in front of the [Atomic Bomb Dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_Bomb_Dome) because of the protests.[^cite_note-204]

### Kyrgyzstan

On 29 October 300 people joined the Palestinian community in [Bishkek](4554.md) in demonstrations funded by an Islamic publisher, [Islamskiy Zhurnal Umma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamskiy_Zhurnal_Umma?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-Eurasianet20231031-205]

### Malaysia



Protest opposed to Israeli action in Gaza, 22 October, Kuala Lumpur

Around 15,000 people gathered in [Kuala Lumpur](16854.md) to express their disapproval of Israel's air attacks and land assault on Gaza. Protesters wore Palestinian keffiyeh scarves and raised placards with the words "Israel cuak" (meaning "Israel is frightened"). Additionally, two effigies covered in the Israeli flag were burned while the crowd chanted "Hidup Palestin" (meaning "long live Palestine") and "Hancur Zionist" (meaning "crush the Zionists").[^cite_note-206] 1,000 Muslims marched in Kuala Lumpur and 5,000 people marched in [Sungai Petani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sungai_Petani) following Friday prayers to demonstrate support for Palestine on 13 October.[^cite_note-aljazeera-207][^cite_note-208] Nearly a thousand people demonstrated outside the US embassy in Kuala Lumpur on 13 January 2024;[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210] waving Palestinian flags.[^cite_note-apnews.com-132] On 12 October, about 7,000 people attended a pro-Palestine march towards the US embassy to mark the one-year anniversary of the Gaza war.[^cite_note-211]

In May 2025, more than 1,000 people marched through the streets of Kuala Lumpur and chanted "Free Palestine" and "Stop the genocide" to mark the 77th anniversary of the [Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba).[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213] On 21 June, thousands of people attended a pro-Palestine rally known as *Himpunan 10K Malam Untukmu Palestin* at the [Sultan Muhammad IV Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultan_Muhammad_IV_Stadium) in [Kota Bharu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kota_Bharu), [Kelantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelantan).[^cite_note-214] On 16 August, more than 70 people gathered at [Dataran Merdeka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Square_\(Kuala_Lumpur\)) to show solidarity and commemorate the [journalists killed in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_journalists_killed_in_the_Gaza_war).[^cite_note-215] On 2 October, more than 3,000 people marched to the US embassy in Kuala Lumpur, demanding the release of Malaysian activists of the [Global Sumud Flotilla](80704925.md) intercepted by Israeli forces.[^cite_note-216] On 10 October, more than 1,000 boats by members of fishing associations in [Tumpat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tumpat_District), Kota Bharu, [Bachok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachok_District), and [Kuala Besut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuala_Besut) took part in a convoy on the [Kelantan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelantan_River) in Kota Bharu to show solidarity with the people of Gaza.[^cite_note-217]

### Maldives

On 13 October 2023, hundreds of Maldivians attended a pro-Palestine rally in [Malé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mal%C3%A9) as a response to Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-218]

### Nepal

On 10 October 2023, demonstrators outside the prime minister's office called for the evacuation of Nepalis in Israel.[^cite_note-219] On 20 October 2023, protesters outside of the Israeli embassy called for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-220] Participants included former Prime Minister [Baburam Bhattarai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baburam_Bhattarai) and [Nepal Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepal_Socialist_Party) leader [Hisila Yami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hisila_Yami).[^cite_note-221] On 30 October 2023, protesters in [Patan Durbar Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patan_Durbar_Square) in [Kathmandu](17168.md) called for a ceasefire, holding banners reading "Indigenous People of Nepal are with Palestine" and "South-South Solidarity".[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] On 31 October 2023, the Human Rights and Peace Society organized a demonstration outside the United Nations office in [Lalitpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lalitpur,_Nepal) calling on the organization to take action to protect civilian lives in Gaza and to release all hostages held by Hamas, including Nepali hostage [Bipin Joshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidnapping_of_Bipin_Joshi).[^cite_note-224] On 29 March 2024, doctors and healthcare workers from the [Maharajgunj Medical Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Medicine,_Nepal) held a protest outside the Israeli embassy, condemning the [killing of over 685 healthcare workers](75657910.md) in Palestine and calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227]

### Pakistan

Further information: [D-Chowk Dharna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D-Chowk_Dharna), [2024 Faizabad sit-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Faizabad_sit-in), and [2025 Tehreek-e-Labbaik Pakistan protests](81307395.md)

See also: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Pakistan)

On 13 October 2023, public gatherings expressing support for Palestine were held following [Friday prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friday_prayers). Various political and religious groups organized numerous protests in major cities such as [Karachi](17123.md), [Lahore](125315.md), [Peshawar](66230.md), and the capital [Islamabad](51153.md). [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_flag) and [Israeli flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_flag) were burned in the demonstrations.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229] On 13 January 2024, As a part of the "global day of action", large crowds gathered in front of the Lahore Press Club to protest against the war and call for a permanent ceasefire.[^cite_note-230]

Thousands of people, comprising women and children, assembled in Islamabad for the biggest pro-Palestine demonstration in Pakistan since the commencement of Israel's conflict with Gaza in October.[^cite_note-231] Demonstrators in Karachi displayed Palestinian flags and donned the keffiyeh during a gathering arranged by [Jamaat-e-Islami Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaat-e-Islami_Pakistan), the nation's biggest religious political group.[^cite_note-232] On 3 August 2024, Jamaat-e-Islami Pakistan arranged another demonstrations in [Rawalpindi](343524.md) which takes place after the assassination of Ismail Haniyeh.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-188]

On 13 April 2025, [Jamaat-e-Islami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaat-e-Islami) organized the [Gaza Solidarity March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Solidarity_March?action=edit\&redlink=1) in [Karachi](17123.md).[^cite_note-233]

### Philippines

In [Mindanao](20546.md), protests were held in [Marawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marawi) in solidarity with the Palestinians on 10 October.[^cite_note-234] On 16 October, 12,000–25,000 protesters took part in the [Bangsamoro](37260549.md) Rally for Free Palestine in [Cotabato City](298955.md). They also called for neighboring Arab countries to open their borders to refugees and for the United States to take a neutral stance on the issue.[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236] Another grand solidarity rally was held on 19 October where various Moro groups in Marawi condemned the [hospital explosion in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion)[^cite_note-237]

Various leftist organizations, including [Bayan Muna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayan_Muna) and [Gabriela Women's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriela_Women's_Party), also launched separate demonstrations in support of Palestine and urged for the end of Israel's occupation.[^cite_note-autogenerated1-238] A demonstration on 31 October held by 500 protesters from [Bagong Alyansang Makabayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagong_Alyansang_Makabayan) at the Israeli embassy in [Taguig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taguig) reiterated support for Palestine, condemnation of genocide and what it called the subservience of the Philippine government to the United States.[^cite_note-239] The protesters at Taguig clashed with local police.[^cite_note-240] In response, Israeli ambassador [Ilan Fluss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilan_Fluss?action=edit\&redlink=1) insisted on Israel's right to defend itself and that the conflict was against Hamas rather than Palestine. He also equated Hamas to [Islamic State](9087364.md).[^cite_note-241] Two hundred people from left-wing groups held another protest at the [US embassy in Manila](10576426.md) on 14 November reiterating their solidarity with Palestinians and accusing the US of bearing "overwhelming responsibility" in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-242] They also clashed with the local police.[^cite_note-243]

Filipino Christians from various churches and organizations held a prayer vigil at the Philippine-Israel Friendship Marker at the [Quezon Memorial Circle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quezon_Memorial_Circle) in [Quezon City](284223.md) in solidarity with Israel and civilian casualties.[^cite_note-244] Students at the [University of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Philippines) protested in support of a ceasefire.[^cite_note-245]

A march was held from [Luneta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luneta) to the [CCP Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Center_of_the_Philippines_Complex) in Manila on 25 November to call for a ceasefire and an end to Israel's siege of Gaza.[^cite_note-246]

### Singapore

On 2 February 2024, two separate events took place in support of Palestine, one of which involved "a group of about 70 people" gathering along [Orchard Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchard_Road) and marching toward [the Istana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Istana) to deliver 130 letters to Prime Minister [Lee Hsien Loong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Hsien_Loong) while carrying umbrellas with watermelon patterns. In response, the police investigated the event as a public assembly organised without a permit, and warned against calls for similar protests at the upcoming [Singapore Airshow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore_Airshow).[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] On 27 June, three women were charged with organising a procession in a prohibited area in relation to the February 2024 Orchard Road march as well as for sending over 140 letters to the [prime minister](350647.md), urging him to [cut ties with Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Singapore_relations).[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250] They were acquitted on 22 October 2025 after a judge ruled that the defendants were unaware that they were holding the event in a prohibited area.[^cite_note-251] Later in April 2026, the three women were later fined S\$3,000 (\$2,300) each by the [High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Court_of_Singapore) for their actions.[^cite_note-252]

On the evening of 15 April, a group of three people held a banner with the text "End SG – Israel arms trade" from the OCBC Skyway at [Gardens by the Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gardens_by_the_Bay).[^cite_note-253]

### South Korea

On 17 October, around 500 Israelis and South Koreans gathered in central Seoul to show solidarity with Israel.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] On 17 November, civic groups in [Seoul](19159283.md) laid out 2,000 pairs of shoes to symbolize the deaths of innocent civilians killed in Gaza, the occupied [West Bank](33209.md) and Israel.[^cite_note-256] On 13 January 2024, as a part of the "global day of action", protestors marched in Seoul demanding a ceasefire and an end to the Israeli war on Gaza.[^cite_note-257] On 20 July dozens of South Koreans in [Incheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incheon) rallied in solidarity with Gaza.[^cite_note-258]

### Sri Lanka

On 13 October, protesters held signs that read "Palestine you will never walk alone".[^cite_note-\:0-172]

On 14 November 159 [Parliament members](571660.md) signed an open letter to UN secretary-general [António Guterres](471806.md) demanding "necessary steps to be imposed on Israel to stop its onslaught, oppressions, and aggressions" against the Palestinian people; they also appealed to Western countries to stop supporting Israel and conform to international law "without hypocrisy and double standard".[^cite_note-259]

In December 2023, protesters expressed disappointment and frustration over their government's choice to dispatch migrant workers to Israel.[^cite_note-260]

### Taiwan

Protests in Taiwan, in support for both Israel and Palestine has been ongoing since the start of the war.[^cite_note-261]

On 13 April 2025, civic and minor political groups in Taiwan such as the [Taiwan Obasang Political Equality Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Obasang_Political_Equality_Party) and the [Green Party Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Party_Taiwan), demonstrated against the war in Gaza. Demonstrators marched to the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Taiwan\)), calling on the government to defend human rights and freedom, issued an official statement condemning Israel's action, terminating all cooperation with Israel and ceased providing technology and materials to Israel for its war on Gaza.[^cite_note-262]

### Thailand

On 22 October 2023, hundreds of [Thai Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_Muslim) and Palestinians protested outside of Israeli embassy in [Bangkok](56615.md) to protest against Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-263] On 19 May 2024, about 60 people joined in a pro-Palestine rally outside the British, German and American embassies to protest of the countries' alleged support for Israel. The protest was organized by THAI for Palestine, the Palestine Solidarity Campaign (PSC) Thailand and Socialist Thai.[^cite_note-264] On 4 October 2025, several pro-Palestine protestors marched through Bangkok, demanding the suspension of Thai labor deployment to Israel, the prompt cancellation of all arms sales and defense agreements with Israel, and the vetting of all Israeli visitors for any links to the IDF.[^cite_note-265]

### Uzbekistan

On 29 October 2023, around 100 people gathered at [Amir Timur Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Timur_Square) in [Tashkent](57533.md) to express solidarity with Palestine. Shortly after the demonstration, more than a hundred people were taken into custody by the police. The majority were shortly released afterwards, but three people were jailed for 15 days.[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-Eurasianet20231031-205]

## Europe



Protest in front of the [European Commission](9974.md) branch in Barcelona, 7 January 2024



Pro-Palestinian protest in central [Stockholm](26741.md), Sweden, 27 January 2024

### Albania

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Albania)

On 20 October 2023, a rally was held in [Skanderbeg Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg_Square) in the capital [Tirana](31075.md) in support of Palestine and the Palestinian people. Protestors condemned Israel's attacks on Palestinians and called for an end to the [Israeli occupation of Palestine](2781576.md) and for the implementation of the [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution).[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268]

In April 2025, a demonstration took place in Tirana against the visit of Albanian Prime Minister [Edi Rama](277805.md) to Israel.[^cite_note-269] In August 2025, some 300 Muslim religious leaders issued an open letter condemning Israeli actions as genocide, called for boycotts of companies involved in the war, solidarity with Palestinians and global action by the Muslim world.[^cite_note-Gjevori-270]

### Austria

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Austria)

On 13 January 2024, a pro-Palestine march was held in [Vienna](55866.md) as part of the "global day of action".[^cite_note-271] Demonstrators carried Palestinian flags and also banners that read: "Hands off Rafah", "Immediate cease-fire" and "Stop the genocide in Gaza".[^cite_note-272] On 29 June 2024, protests were held in Vienna carrying Palestinian flags and banners and demanding an end to the bombardment of Gaza.[^cite_note-273]

### Belgium

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Belgium)

On 10 December 2023, about 4,000 people in Brussels waved Belgian flags and demonstrated against [antisemitism](1078.md) amid concern over the rising number of antisemitic incidents in Europe from the Gaza war.[^cite_note-\:12-21] On 18 December, 27,000 demonstrators marched in Brussels on a national march calling for an immediate and permanent ceasefire in Gaza.[^cite_note-274]

On 21 January 2024, protesters in [Brussels](3708.md) demonstrated against the war and called for a permanent ceasefire.[^cite_note-275] Ahead of a foreign ministers meeting in Brussels, a group of 100 prominent people, including former Irish president [Mary Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Robinson) and former Swedish foreign minister [Margot Wallström](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margot_Wallstr%C3%B6m), called on the EU to prevent the "unprecedented rate of civilian killing" in Gaza.[^cite_note-276]

In January 2025, around 7,000 demonstrators marched on the streets in Brussels, calling for a permanent ceasefire in Gaza and sanctions against Israel.[^cite_note-277] Another pro-Palestine demonstration was carried out in Brussels on 7 September, with between 70,000, and 120,000 in attendance.[^cite_note-278]

### Bosnia and Herzegovina

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina)

On 22 October, thousands marched in [Sarajevo](26786.md) in support of Gaza, chanting, "Yesterday [Srebrenica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srebrenica), today Gaza", referring to the [1995 massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srebrenica_massacre) of 8,000 Muslim men and boys.[^cite_note-279] Several thousand people gathered in Sarajevo, waving Palestinian and [Bosnian flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_flag) and demanding a halt to the [Israeli offensive into Gaza](74998519.md).[^cite_note-280]

On 7 July 2024, demonstrations were held in [Mostar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostar), denouncing the [genocide in Gaza](75668427.md); demonstrators placed a Palestinian flag on the [Stari Most](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stari_Most) over the [Neretva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neretva) river.[^cite_note-281]

### Bulgaria

On 13 October 2023, Bulgarians and Palestinians gathered in front of the [National Palace of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Palace_of_Culture) in [Sofia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia) to express solidarity with Palestinians, despite the city council banning the protest.[^cite_note-282]

### Croatia

In April 2024, pro-Palestinian demonstrators staged a protest in front of the German Embassy in [Zagreb](55906.md) over the German government's perceived complicity in [Israel's killing of Palestinians in Gaza](75668427.md).[^cite_note-283]

### Cyprus

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Cyprus)

On 15 October, a pro-Palestinian protest was held in [Larnaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larnaca).[^cite_note-284] On 19 October, a protest in support of Gaza was held in [Nicosia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicosia).[^cite_note-285] On 20 October, another pro-Palestinian protest was organised by the Cyprus Peace Council, attended by 2,000 protestors as well as MPs, mayors, the general secretary of [AKEL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AKEL) and the Palestinian ambassador to Cyprus.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] On 17 October, a pro-Israel rally was organised by the Israeli community.[^cite_note-288] Protests were held at the UK's [RAF Akrotiri](432784.md) base on 14 and 15 January 2024 after the base was used to launch airstrikes on Yemen.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] The protesters carried a banner demanding a "Ceasefire" while another read "Stop funding genocide".[^cite_note-291]

### Czech Republic

On 20 January 2024, between 1,200 and 1,400 people marched through the streets of [Prague](23844.md) in support of Palestine.[^cite_note-292] Another pro-Palestinian protest was held on 23 July, where several protestors blocked the entrance of the Representation of the European Commission in the Czech Republic, demanding a ceasefire in Gaza.[^cite_note-293]

In March 2026, a warehouse in [Pardubice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pardubice) owned by drone manufacturer LPP Holding was destroyed in an [arson attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Pardubice_arson_attack). No injuries were reported. The Earthquake Faction claimed responsibility for the attack, stating that it targeted the facility because of LPP Holding's planned cooperation with Israeli defence company [Elbit Systems](1341669.md). The group described the attack as a protest against Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-294] Authorities later detained ten suspects in several countries and charged them with [terrorism](30636.md)-related offenses carrying penalties of up to 20 years' imprisonment.[^cite_note-295]

### Denmark



"Jews for a free Palestine" banner in Copenhagen, 2 February 2024

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Denmark)

Protesters marched in support of Gaza in [Copenhagen](5166.md) on 2 February 2024, chanting "Free Palestine" and "Palestine will never die".[^cite_note-296] The protesters called for an end to Israel's military attacks in Gaza. They also described the conflict as a genocide and urged politicians to step up their efforts in aiding the Palestinian populace.[^cite_note-297]

On 6 October 2024, people rallied in Copenhagen in solidarity with Palestine and Lebanon during Israel's incursion into Lebanese territory.[^cite_note-ReferenceB-189]

On 24 August 2025, more than 10,000 people joined a pro-Palestine demonstration in Copenhagen, calling for an end of the war and urging the government to [recognise Palestine as a state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_Palestine).[^cite_note-298]

### Estonia

On 5 November 2023, a pro-Palestine demonstration was peacefully held in [Tallinn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallinn) to express support of Palestine and calling for the Estonian government to condemn Israel's actions.[^cite_note-299]

### Finland



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Helsinki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki), Finland, 21 October 2023

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Finland)

On 19 November 2023, an estimated 4,000 people participated in a march in support of the Palestinian people in [Helsinki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki). The march also called for an end to Israeli military action in Gaza and criticised the Finnish government's agreement to purchase a missile defense system from Israel.[^cite_note-300] In January 2024, 79 Finnish diplomats signed a letter to Foreign Minister [Elina Valtonen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elina_Valtonen), criticising the official response to the Israeli attacks on Gaza.[^cite_note-301] In February 2024, it was reported that participants would gather in [Mannerheimintie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mannerheimintie) in Helsinki to protest, although authorities had not granted permission for any demonstrations.[^cite_note-302]

### France

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_France)

Further information: [March for the Republic and Against Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_the_Republic_and_Against_Antisemitism) and [2024 pro-Palestinian protests at Sciences Po](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_at_Sciences_Po)

In the night preceding 12 October 2023, [police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_police) fired tear gas and water cannons to disperse hundreds of pro-Palestinian demonstrators in [Paris](22989.md)[^cite_note-WP13Oct-303] on a banned pro-Palestinian rally.[^cite_note-\:0-172]

On Thursday, 12 October 2023, in response to former Hamas leader [Khaled Mashal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_Mashal) calling Muslims worldwide to stage protests in support of Palestinians in a "day of rage" on Friday 13 October, the French Interior Minister [Gérald Darmanin](54070416.md) imposed a ban on pro-Palestinian demonstrations because he expected such rallies to "disrupt public order".[^cite_note-WP13Oct-303] The same day, the [Representative Council of French Jewish Institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_Repr%C3%A9sentatif_des_Institutions_juives_de_France) organized a pro-Israel rally.[^cite_note-304] After Darmanin's ban on pro-Palestinian demonstrations, French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md), in a televised national address on the same day, urged restraint to France's Muslims and Jews,[^cite_note-WP13Oct-303] urging all citizens to refrain from bringing the conflict to France.[^cite_note-\:0-172]



Demonstration 11 November 2023 to cease fire in Gaza in [Paris](22989.md), France



[March for the Republic and Against Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_the_Republic_and_Against_Antisemitism) in Paris, 12 November

On 14 October 2023, French-Algerian journalist [Taha Bouhafs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taha_Bouhafs?action=edit\&redlink=1) was arrested while covering a pro-Palestine protest in Paris. He told [+972 Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/+972_Magazine) that people were getting "strangled by the police", and that the police fined him for participating in an "illegal demonstration" despite showing his [press card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_card). He also claimed that the police threatened to break his legs if they saw him again at a protest.[^cite_note-305]

On 22 October 2023, France held its first authorized pro-Palestine rally, drawing 15,000 participants who chanted: "Gaza, Paris is with you".[^cite_note-306] On 12 November, over 100,000 people protested in response to the recent [rise in antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_France) in Paris. Prime Minister [Élisabeth Borne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lisabeth_Borne), the heads of France's upper and lower houses of parliament, former presidents [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande) and [Nicolas Sarkozy](557007.md), and several leading politicians joined the demonstrators. The presence of the far-right [National Rally](67119.md) leader [Marine Le Pen](572897.md), whose party has a history of antisemitism, caused criticism from some participants such as Borne, who is the daughter of [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust) survivors.[^cite_note-307]

On 21 January 2024, Paris police stopped and dispersed an automobile parade waving Palestinian flags.[^cite_note-308] On 22 January, MP [Louis Boyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Boyard) was verbally assaulted and threatened by a pro-Israeli activist.[^cite_note-309] A demonstration of hundreds of protestors occurred in [Marseille](40888948.md) in support of Palestine on 18 February.[^cite_note-310]

On 25 April 2024, students from [Sorbonne University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorbonne_University) called on the French government to help Palestinians.[^cite_note-311] On 15, May people gathered around the Sorbonne to commemorate [Nakba Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba_Day).[^cite_note-312]

On 13 November 2024, violent protests erupted in Paris during a far-right pro-Israel [gala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gala_\(festivity\)) that Israeli Finance Minister Bezalel Smotrich was scheduled to attend.[^cite_note-313] The protests occurred on the eve of a [UEFA](232175.md) match featuring visiting Israeli team [Maccabi Tel Aviv F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabi_Tel_Aviv_F.C.), less than one week after [riots during and after their last game in Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2024_Amsterdam_riots) caused an international incident.[^cite_note-314] Scuffles and altercations occurred during the match.[^cite_note-315]

On 25 May 2025, nearly 4,000 people gathered at the [Place de la République](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_de_la_R%C3%A9publique) in Paris to show support for the Palestinians.[^cite_note-316]

On 6 November 2025, four people were detained for disrupting a concert by the [Israel Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Philharmonic_Orchestra) at the [Philharmonie de Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philharmonie_de_Paris) as part of protest actions against the war.[^cite_note-317]

### Germany



Pro-Israel protest in [Hanover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanover), 10 October



Pro-Palestine protest in Berlin, 4 November

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Germany)

Since October 2023, many pro-Palestine demonstrations were held in Germany. Most of them were peaceful. Some participants voiced support for Hamas or other Islamist organizations, called for the destruction of Israel, or used antisemitic slogans.[^cite_note-318] In [Berlin](3354.md), authorities banned a pro-Palestinian rally from being held.[^cite_note-DWVideo20231013-319] A number of spontaneous demonstrations protesting the bombing of Gaza took place across the country.[^cite_note-320] Germany banned fundraising for Hamas.[^cite_note-GCGSN-321]

On 22 October, a pro-Israel rally was held in Berlin at the [Brandenburg Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandenburg_Gate). President [Frank-Walter Steinmeier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank-Walter_Steinmeier) was present at the demonstration, as well as representatives from the [German-Israeli Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German-Israeli_Society), most mainstream political parties, the Council of the [Protestant Church in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant_Church_in_Germany), the [German Bishops' Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Bishops'_Conference), the [Central Council of Jews in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Council_of_Jews_in_Germany), the [Federation of German Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_of_German_Industries), the [German Trade Union Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Trade_Union_Confederation) and the Israeli ambassador [Ron Prosor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Prosor).[^cite_note-322]

On 24 February 2024, protesters in Berlin carried a sign reading, "Stop the Genocide".[^cite_note-323] In [Neukölln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuk%C3%B6lln), a neighborhood of Berlin, pro-Palestinian protesters described police crackdowns on protest that were "shocking and violent".[^cite_note-324] In late April, a camp was set up in Berlin in front of the [Bundestag](3768.md) in opposition to arms transfers to Israel.[^cite_note-AJE_proPalestine_second_week-325]

On 15 July, clashes broke out between police and over 600 pro-Palestinian protestors, Seven demonstrators and 17 police officers were hurt and 28 demonstrators were charged.[^cite_note-326] On 25 July, protests were held in Berlin, during which demonstrators recreated a scene from the Israeli bombardment of Gaza.[^cite_note-327]

On 30 September, ahead of anniversary demonstrations, several activists were detained in Berlin.[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329]

On 6 October 2024, people rallied in Berlin in solidarity with Palestine and Lebanon during Israel's incursion.[^cite_note-ReferenceB-189][^cite_note-330]

On 15 May 2025, about 1,100 people took part in a pro-Palestine demonstration in [Kreuzberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kreuzberg), Berlin to commemorate the 77th anniversary of the Nakba and to protest against Israel's military operation in Gaza. During the protests, several protesters and police officers were injured in violent confrontations.[^cite_note-331]

On 10 September, during a pro-Palestine protest in Berlin's [Mitte district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitte), a member of the [Irish Bloc Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Bloc_Berlin?action=edit\&redlink=1) was assaulted by German police. The incident was condemned by the Irish [Taoiseach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoiseach), [Micheál Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miche%C3%A1l_Martin).[^cite_note-332] On 27 September, more than 100,000 people joined a pro-Palestine rally in Berlin to protest against Germany's support for Israel.[^cite_note-333]

### Greece



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Chania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chania), Greece on 5 October 2024

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Greece)

On 12 October 2023, 200 demonstrators gathered at [Syntagma Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syntagma_Square) in [Athens](1216.md) to show solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-334] A day later, 2,000 protestors including Palestinians, members of Muslim communities, left-wing and anarchist groups marched toward [the Israeli embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Israel,_Athens).[^cite_note-aa-335] On 29 October, a crowd of 5,000 demonstrated in Athens, calling for an end to the Gaza massacre.[^cite_note-336] On 25 March 2024, pro-Palestinian protesters blocked a tank during a military parade for [Greece's Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebration_of_the_Greek_Revolution) in Athens.[^cite_note-337] On 15 May, a crowd of 2,500 people marched in Athens towards the Israeli embassy in solidarity of Palestine; the protesters later clashed with [police](10063533.md).[^cite_note-338]

On 22 July 2025, more than 300 people on the island of [Syros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syros) protested against the docking of the [MS Crown Iris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MS_Crown_Iris) which is owned by Israeli cruise company, [Mano Maritime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mano_Maritime). The ship, which was carrying more than 1,600 passengers, was later rerouted to Cyprus due to "safety concerns".[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] On 24 August, hundreds of people joined at a pro-Palestine march at [White Tower Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Tower_of_Thessaloniki) in [Thessaloniki](40471.md) to protest against Israel's attacks in Gaza.[^cite_note-341]

### Hungary

In August 2024, a pro-Palestine protest was held in front of the US embassy in [Budapest](36787.md) in light of the International Day of the World's Indigenous Peoples. This was the first time that a pro-Palestine protest was allowed to be held in Hungary since October 2023.[^cite_note-342] Hungary has also imposed restrictions on pro-Palestinian protests since 7 October.[^cite_note-ineurf-24]

### Iceland

Pro-Palestinian protests were held at [Austurvöllur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austurv%C3%B6llur) in [Reykjavík](25798.md).[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344]

### Ireland

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Ireland)



Pro-Palestine protest in [Dublin](8504.md), Ireland, 18 November 2023

Thousands of people marched in cities and towns across Ireland, including [Carlow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlow), [Cork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cork_\(city\)), [Dublin](8504.md), [Ennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ennis), [Galway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galway) and [Limerick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limerick) in support of Palestine and against Israel's attacks on Gaza and the continuing occupation of Palestine.[^cite_note-The_Journal-345][^cite_note-The_Irish_Times-346] Residents of [Ballina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballina,_County_Mayo), the ancestral hometown of US president [Joe Biden](145422.md), splashed red paint and wrote "[Genocide Joe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_Joe)" over a mural of the president's face.[^cite_note-347]

### Italy

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Italy)



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Avezzano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avezzano), Italy, 22 September 2025

Thousands of pro-Palestinian demonstrators marched in [Rome](25458.md), carrying a large Palestinian flag and chanting slogans in support of Palestine.[^cite_note-aa-335] On 17 November, a long Palestinian flag was hung from the [Leaning Tower of Pisa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leaning_Tower_of_Pisa) during a pro-Palestinian protest in the [city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pisa).[^cite_note-348] On 27 January 2024, in [Milan](36511.md), police clashed with pro-Palestinian protesters despite an official ban on protests on [International Holocaust Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Holocaust_Remembrance_Day).[^cite_note-349] On 28 May 2024, a group of pro-Palestine protesters occupied a train station in [Bologna](21069333.md).[^cite_note-350] On 5 June 2024, a 15-meter Palestinian flag was unfurled on top of [Milan Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milan_Cathedral) by former Italian MP [Stefano Apuzzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefano_Apuzzo?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-351] On 5 October 2024, a Pro-Palestine protest turned violent when thousands of protestors clashed with [local police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_police) resulting in at least 34 people injured. Chants of "Free Palestine", "Criminal Israel" and "Now Intifada" rang out in Piazzale Ostiense during the event.[^cite_note-352] On 7 June 2025, around 300,000 people marched through the streets of Rome to protest against Israel's war in Gaza.[^cite_note-353] On 21 June 2025, over 100,000 people attended a protest in Rome against "war, rearmament, genocide, \[and] authoritarianism."[^cite_note-354] In August 2025, thousands of people joined a pro-Palestine protest at the [82nd Venice International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/82nd_Venice_International_Film_Festival).[^cite_note-355]

On 22 September 2025, a [nationwide strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2025_Italian_general_strike_for_Gaza) involving hundreds of thousands of people caused major disruptions across multiple cities.[^cite_note-356]

On 8 May 2026, pavilions at the [61st Venice Biennale](78508191.md) closed after artists went on strike in protest of Israel's participation in the event. The protest was organized by the Art Not Genocide Alliance.[^cite_note-357]

### Latvia
