A pro-Palestine procession planned on 6 January 2024 by the "For a Free Palestine" movement ([Latvian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_language): *Kustība "Par brīvu Palestīnu"*) was prohibited by the [Riga City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riga_City_Council) due to a perceived potential threat to [public safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_safety).[^cite_note-358]

On 1 August 2025, a [protest march](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protest_march) organized by a private individual against Latvia's foreign policy in support of Israel against Palestine took place from [Dailes Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dailes_Theatre) to the square in front of the [Riga Congress House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riga_Congress_House?action=edit\&redlink=1). At the same time, a [picket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picketing) "condemning the genocide and violence of [radical Islamists](8734632.md) against Jews, [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze) and other [ethnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_minorities_in_the_Middle_East) and [religious minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_minorities_in_the_Middle_East) in the [Middle East](19323.md)" organized by the association Jewnited.lv took place in front of the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Latvia\)).[^cite_note-359]

During Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md)'s visit to Latvia on 5 August, a pro-Palestinian rally with approximately 30 participants was held near [Riga Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riga_Castle) protesting against his visit and demanding Israel to stop [military operations](74998519.md) in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md). A simultaneous pro-Israel [counter-protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-protest) was organized on the other side of the square.[^cite_note-360]

### Lithuania

On 29 November 2023, around 100 people gathered in [Vilnius](32597.md) to protest Israel's actions in Gaza, marking the International Day of Solidarity with the Palestinian People. At the same time, a pro-Israel protest was held to counter the pro-Palestine protesters.[^cite_note-361]

### Luxembourg

On 13 January 2024, as a part of the "global day of action", protestors gathered in front of the [US embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Embassy_Luxembourg) in [Luxembourg City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourg_City) to demand an end to the Israeli war in Gaza and a permanent ceasefire.[^cite_note-362]

### Malta

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Malta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Malta)

On 6 October 2024, a pro-Palestine protest was held in [Valletta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valletta), marking the one-year anniversary of the war and demanding a ceasefire in Gaza and to 'end Zionist terror' against the Palestinians.[^cite_note-363]

### Moldova

On 19 October 2023, over 20 protesters gathered in [Chișinău](56635.md) in front of the [Parliament of Moldova](2818427.md), demanding the chamber to condemn the [Gaza genocide](75668427.md) and the recognition of an independent Palestinian state.[^cite_note-364]

### Netherlands

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_the_Netherlands)

Further information: [2024 pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses in the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses_in_the_Netherlands) and [2025 pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses in the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses_in_the_Netherlands)

See also: [2024 University of Amsterdam pro-Palestinian campus occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_University_of_Amsterdam_pro-Palestinian_campus_occupation)



Anti-war demonstrations with Palestinian flags in Amsterdam, the Netherlands

A group of students rallied in [The Hague](30269.md) on 13 October 2023 to show solidarity with Palestine,[^cite_note-aljazeera-207] followed by a 15,000-strong pro-Palestinian protest in [Amsterdam](844.md) on 15 October.[^cite_note-365] On 23 October, activists opposed to Israel's actions in Gaza occupied the entrance to the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) in The Hague, demanding action against [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) for alleged war crimes.[^cite_note-Activists_occupy-366] On 21 December, civil servants demanded a truce.[^cite_note-367] In January 2024, a billboard campaign displayed messages regarding the war, such as "every ten minutes one Palestinian child dies".[^cite_note-368] On 13 January, a protest organised by the [Plant een Olijfboom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plant_een_Olijfboom_foundation?action=edit\&redlink=1) (Plant an Olive Tree) Foundation was held in [Amsterdam](844.md), honoring children killed in Gaza by placing around 10,000 pairs of children's shoes in [Dam Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dam_Square), each pair representing one Palestinian child killed by Israeli air strikes.[^cite_note-369]

On 11 January 2024, hundreds of pro-Israel protesters carrying Dutch and Israeli flags gathered outside the International Court of Justice. Hundreds of Palestinian supporters also gathered to watch the genocide hearings on a large screen less than a hundred metres away from the pro-Israel group.[^cite_note-370] A healthcare workers collective protested against [Gaza's healthcare collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_Strip_healthcare_collapse) with five demands: an end to Israel's [bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Gaza) of hospitals, an end to the [killing of healthcare workers](75657910.md), the end of arms sales to Israel, the release of [abducted healthcare workers](75234504.md) and immediate access to medical supplies.[^cite_note-371] During [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md)'s visit to the National Holocaust Museum in Amsterdam, protesters at a square nearby chanted "[Never again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Never_again) is now" and "Ceasefire now" and held signs that said "Jews against genocide".[^cite_note-372]

On 17 March 2024, people lined up thousands of shoes dedicated to the 13,000 children who died from the war in a public square in [Utrecht](50333.md).[^cite_note-373]



Protesters reinforcing a barricade at the [University of Amsterdam campus occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_University_of_Amsterdam_pro-Palestinian_campus_occupation), 8 May 2024

On 8 May 2024, as part of a series of [pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses), police broke up a student protest at the [University of Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Amsterdam), where protesters had blockaded the university grounds. Protesters were demanding an end to the conflict, with some calling on the university to sever academic ties to Israel. Confrontations were described as violent, but it was unclear whether anyone had been injured or detained.[^cite_note-AP_9_May_2024_NL-374] Reuters reported that police struck protesters on the head with batons.[^cite_note-Reuters_9_May_2024_NL-375]

On 20 April 2025, more than 10,000 people showed up in a pro-Palestinian demonstration in [Rotterdam](26049.md) to protest against [Israel's actions on Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes_in_the_Gaza_war).[^cite_note-376] On 18 May, more than 100,000 people marched through The Hague at the [red line protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_line_demonstration) while wearing red clothing as an expression to "draw a red line" against Israel's blockade on Gaza and demanded the Dutch government to take more action against Israel.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] On 15 June, the same initiative was repeated and reached 150,000 protesters.[^cite_note-379] On 29 June, around 18,000 shoes and toys were displayed in [Almere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almere), representing more than 17,400 children that were killed in Gaza.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381]

Hundreds of people protested during the [2025 NATO summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_The_Hague_NATO_summit) in The Hague against Israel's war in the Gaza Strip and the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) following [US strikes on Iranian nuclear sites](80262844.md).[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383]

On 5 October 2025, the third red line demonstration was held. With 250,000 participants, this was the largest demonstration in the country in over 20 years. Five activists were arrested.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386]

In April 2026, a public installation organised by the Plant an Olive Tree Foundation, in [Dam Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dam_Square), [Amsterdam](844.md), featured rows of children’s shoes to commemorate Palestinian children killed during the Gaza war. The display took place shortly before Holocaust Remembrance Day.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389] The [Combat Antisemitism Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_Antisemitism_Movement), a monitoring organisation, criticised it saying "By adopting symbols inseparable from the Nazi genocide, the display recasts the Gaza war through the lens of Jewish extermination".[^cite_note-390] *[The Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel)* wrote that the installation "appropriate Holocaust imagery by using shoes to symbolize the Palestinian victims".[^cite_note-391]

#### Football riots

Main article: [November 2024 Amsterdam riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2024_Amsterdam_riots)

On 2 November 2024, a pro-Palestinian protester was attacked by [AFC Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Ajax) supporters near [Amsterdam Centraal station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amsterdam_Centraal_station). He was one of several pro-Palestinian protesters who had called for a boycott of [Maccabi Tel Aviv F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabi_Tel_Aviv_F.C.) and other Israeli teams to match the [boycott of Russian teams](70202720.md#Sports).[^cite_note-Volkskrant3-392]

On 7 November 2024, [violence erupted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2024_Amsterdam_riots) between supporters of Maccabi Tel Aviv, who were in Amsterdam to compete in a [UEFA Europa League](232175.md) game, and locals after the Israeli football team was filmed making [anti-Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Palestinian_racism) and [anti-Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Arab_racism) chants, tearing down Palestinian flags from homes and attacking Moroccan taxi drivers. Benjamin Netanyahu and Israeli commentators said the attacks on Israeli fans were [antisemitic](1078.md) and compared them to a [pogrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogrom). Dutch Prime Minister [Dick Schoof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Schoof) and Amsterdam Mayor [Femke Halsema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Femke_Halsema) concurred and issued a three-day ban on protests in the city.[^cite_note-393] The actions of the Maccabi Tel Aviv fans were meanwhile described as "[hooliganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hooliganism)" and racist by the local authorities and the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md), respectively.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-\:9-395]

On 13 November 2024, video emerged showing police beating pro-Palestinian protesters defying the ongoing city-wide ban.[^cite_note-396] Halsema said the video "looks serious".[^cite_note-397]

### North Macedonia

On 20 October, citizens of varying ethnicities, such as [Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians_in_North_Macedonia), [Macedonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonians_\(ethnic_group\)), [Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turks_in_North_Macedonia), [Bosnian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosniaks_in_North_Macedonia) and [Romani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people_in_North_Macedonia), held a protest in the [old part](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Bazaar,_Skopje) of the capital [Skopje](29618.md) to show support and solidarity for the Palestinian people under the slogan "Don't be silent about the massacre".[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400]

On 9 August 2025, protesters gathered in front of [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assembly_of_North_Macedonia) in Skopje in solidarity with Palestinians.[^cite_note-401]

### Norway



Rally in support of the people of Gaza, Oslo, Norway, 14 October

On 14 October, thousands of pro-Palestine demonstrators protested outside the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](1518771.md) in [Oslo](22309.md).[^cite_note-402] On 17 October, about 100 pro-Palestine demonstrators protested outside the Israeli embassy in Oslo shouting "[Allahu akbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allahu_akbar)".[^cite_note-403]

On 15 October, about 500 people attended a pro-Israel demonstration in Oslo arranged by [With Israel for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/With_Israel_for_Peace). Among the speakers were politicians such as [Progress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progress_Party_\(Norway\)) leader [Sylvi Listhaug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvi_Listhaug).[^cite_note-404]

On 4 November, 9,000 people attended a pro-Gaza demonstration appealed by [Socialist Left Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Left_Party_\(Norway\)) leader [Kirsti Bergstø](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirsti_Bergst%C3%B8) and [Red Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Party_\(Norway\)) leader [Marie Sneve Martinussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Sneve_Martinussen).[^cite_note-405]

On 11 November, up to 5,000 people attended a demonstration walking from the [Storting](172897.md). The protest was organized in opposition to Norway's role in the conflict.[^cite_note-406]

On 12 August 2024, a protest occurred outside [Norges Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norges_Bank) in Oslo. An elderly man was filmed punching a woman protester during the event.[^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408]

### Poland



Pro-Palestine protest in [Gdańsk](12099.md), Poland, 19 May 2024

Hundreds of demonstrators gathered in [Warsaw](32908.md) on 30 October to express their support for the Palestinian population and condemn the Israeli assaults on Gaza.[^cite_note-409] In November 2023, Warsaw mayor [Rafał Trzaskowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafa%C5%82_Trzaskowski) banned a pro-Palestinian protest in Warsaw,[^cite_note-410] citing security concerns and [antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_Poland).[^cite_note-411] On 23 January 2024, one person protested against the [alleged Israeli genocide of Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation) at the Israeli embassy in Warsaw.[^cite_note-412]

### Portugal

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Portugal)



Pro-Palestine protest in [Lisbon](18091.md), Portugal, 29 October 2023

A pro-Palestine protest was held in [Lisbon](18091.md). A number of people assembled in Lisbon over the weekend to denounce what they referred to as "the genocide in the Gaza Strip" and to call for an urgent cessation of hostilities. The protest kicked off at the US embassy in Lisbon, located in the Sete Rios district, before proceeding to the Israeli embassy in [Avenidas Novas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenidas_Novas).[^cite_note-413] The Portuguese Council for Peace and Cooperation (CPPC), the General Confederation of Portuguese Workers – National Trade Union (CGTP-IN), the Movement for the Rights of the Palestinian People and for Peace in the Middle East (MPPM), and the Noise Project – Youth Association jointly organized a protest advocating for "peace in the Middle East."[^cite_note-414]

### Romania

On 12 October 2023, a pro-Israel rally was organised in [Bucharest](36877.md) by the [Romanian Jewish community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Jews) and the Israeli Embassy. It was attended by more than 600 people,[^cite_note-415] including Health Minister [Alexandru Rafila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandru_Rafila),[^cite_note-416] former Defence Minister [Vasile Dîncu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasile_D%C3%AEncu)[^cite_note-417] and opposition leader [George Simion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Simion).[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419]



A pro-Palestinian rally in the centre of [Bucharest](36877.md), [Romania](25445.md) being escorted by the police, with banners calling for an end to the genocide (May 2024)

On 21 October 2023, a pro-Palestine rally was held in Bucharest and attended by over 1,000 demonstrators,[^cite_note-420] including [Twitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitch_\(service\)) streamer and left-wing activist [Silviu Istrate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silviu_Istrate?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-421]

A pro-Palestinian rally held in Bucharest on 19 May 2024 was attended by Romanian [Greens/EFA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greens/EFA) [MEP](258909.md) [Nicu Ștefănuță](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicu_%C8%98tef%C4%83nu%C8%9B%C4%83).[^cite_note-422]

On 20 June 2024, the administration of the [University of Bucharest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Bucharest) called the [Gendarmerie](4536584.md) to evacuate pro-Palestine protesters occupying its campus. MEP Nicu Ștefănuță compared this event with the invitation of miners by President [Ion Iliescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_Iliescu) in 1990, which resulted in the infamous [June 1990 Mineriad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_1990_Mineriad).[^cite_note-423]

During the 2024 edition of the [Bucharest Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucharest_Pride), some pro-Palestinians shouted "there is no pride in [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza)".[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425]

On [Good Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Friday), 18 April 2025, the Romania-Palestine Solidarity Association organized a [candlelight vigil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candlelight_vigil) at the [Memorial of Rebirth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_of_Rebirth) in the centre of Bucharest to commemorate the victims of the genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-426]

### Russia

Main article: [2023 antisemitic riots in the North Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_antisemitic_riots_in_the_North_Caucasus)

In late October 2023, an anti-Israel mob[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] stormed [Uytash Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uytash_Airport) in [Makhachkala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makhachkala), [Dagestan](751099.md) to prevent the landing of a flight from [Tel Aviv](31453.md), which the mob alleged contained Jewish refugees from Israel arriving in Dagestan.[^cite_note-Meduza-429] These rumors were disseminated by a [Telegram](41501305.md) channel associated with Russian-Ukrainian politician [Ilya Ponomarev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilya_Ponomarev).[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431] 20 people were injured, among them nine police officers, of whom two were injured seriously.[^cite_note-432] The passengers on the plane were unharmed. 150 suspects were identified, while 60 were detained.[^cite_note-433]

The head of Dagestan, [Sergey Melikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergey_Melikov), called the incident a gross violation of the law.[^cite_note-\:2-434] Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) ordered a meeting of his top security officials, while his spokesperson [Dmitry Peskov](9214683.md) blamed the unrest on "outside interference".[^cite_note-435] Putin added that any normal person would be outraged by images of "bloodied children" in Gaza, but Russians needed "to keep a clear head".[^cite_note-436] The Israeli government called on Russian authorities to protect Israelis and [Jews in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews_in_Russia).[^cite_note-\:2-434]

### Serbia

On 13 October, pro-Palestinian protests were organised in [Novi Pazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Pazar) and [Novi Sad](113288.md).[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438] Over 15,000 protesters gathered in Novi Sad expressing solidarity with Palestine and denouncing Israel, with slogans such as "Freedom for Palestine," "From the river to the sea, Palestine will be free," and "Stop the genocide."[^cite_note-439]

### Slovenia

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Slovenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Slovenia)



Pro-Palestine protest in [Ljubljana](42374.md), Slovenia, 13 October 2023

On 13 October, pro-Palestinian protests were organised in [Ljubljana](42374.md). On 9 November, a large crowd gathered in Ljubljana to show their support for Palestinians, while the parliamentary Foreign Policy Committee approved a statement urging for an immediate halt to hostilities, a ceasefire, and the establishment of humanitarian corridors in Gaza.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441]

### Spain

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Spain)



Pro-Israel protest in [Barcelona](4443.md), 15 October 2023

On 9 October, pro-Palestinian demonstrators gathered in the [Puerta del Sol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_del_Sol) in [Madrid](41188263.md).[^cite_note-apnews-442] On 13 October, protestors rallied at the [Columbus Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus_Monument,_Barcelona) in [Barcelona](4443.md).[^cite_note-aljazeera-207] On 8 December, over 3000 pro-Palestine protesters in [Guernica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernica) formed a massive mosaic with a human chain depicting the Palestinian flag to express their solidarity.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444] On 27 January 2024, an estimated 20,000 marched in support of Palestine in Madrid.[^cite_note-445] At least six cabinet ministers joined a march for a ceasefire in Madrid.[^cite_note-446] On 11 May, about 4,000 people participated in pro-Palestine protests in Madrid.[^cite_note-447] On 19 May, mass demonstrations erupted in [Valencia](63861.md) calling for the end of the war.[^cite_note-448] The demonstration also saw the participation of six ministers from Prime Minister Pedro Sanchez's cabinet.[^cite_note-449]

In January 2025, several people held demonstrations against the arrival of the [Maccabi Tel Aviv basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabi_Tel_Aviv_B.C.) in Madrid.[^cite_note-450] On 22 February, around 1,000 people demonstrated in front of the American Embassy in Madrid to protest against the [Gaza genocide](75668427.md) and US's plan to take over Gaza.[^cite_note-451] On 14 June, about 25,000 people marched through the streets of Barcelona calling for an end of the ['genocide' in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation).[^cite_note-452]



Pro-Palestinian protest in Barcelona, 21 October 2023

During the 11th stage of the [2025 Vuelta a España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Vuelta_a_Espa%C3%B1a) on 3 September, a group of pro-Palestine protesters tried to enter the racetrack as the [Israel–Premier Tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Premier_Tech) team passed by in [Bilbao](68029.md), forcing the organizers to finish the segment prematurely without declaring a winner.[^cite_note-453] The incident also forced Israel–Premier Tech to issue new uniforms to its cyclists that did not carry its name.[^cite_note-454] During the 15th stage on 7 September, another pro-Palestine protester tried to enter a road while carrying a Palestinian flag, causing cyclists [Javier Romo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Romo) and [Edward Planckaert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Planckaert) to crash.[^cite_note-455] Romo subsequently withdrew from the race altogether. On 9 September, the 16th stage was shortened by 8 km (5.0 mi) after pro-Palestine protesters gathered in large numbers near the original finish line.[^cite_note-456] On 14 September, thousands of demonstrators tore down barricades at the final finishing circuit of the race and forced the organized to cancel the competition completely. More than 2,200 police officers were drafted as security. Two protesters were arrested and 22 officers were injured. The [Vuelta a España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuelta_a_Espa%C3%B1a) is the final stage of the [Grand Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Tour_\(cycling\)).[^cite_note-457][^cite_note-458]



Pro-Palestinian protest in Madrid, 4 October 2025

Over the weekend of 3-5 October 2025, a wave of pro-Palestine demonstrations swept across Spain, with protests held in dozens of cities over the interception of the [Global Sumud Flotilla](80704925.md) and calls for a halt to Israel’s assault on Gaza. On 4 October, thousands of people took to the streets of Madrid from [Atocha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atocha_\(Madrid\)) to [Callao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Callao_Square) to demand “stop the genocide” and an end to state complicity with the State of Israel. At that time, members of the flotilla were still detained in Israel. According to the Government Delegation, approximately 92,000 people participated, waving Palestinian flags, shouting “Free Palestine” and “Stop the genocide,” and calling for the suspension of arms trade and diplomatic ties with Israel.[^cite_note-459]

### Sweden

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Sweden)

Numerous demonstrations have been held in Sweden since the war began, both pro-Palestinian and pro-Israeli.[^cite_note-460]

On 7 October 2023 – following the attack against Israel – there were pro-Palestinian celebrations in some Swedish cities.[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-\:8-462] In [Malmö](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malm%C3%B6), people drove around in cars waving Palestinian flags and shot fireworks. Similar events occurred in [Helsingborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsingborg), [Kristianstad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristianstad) and [Växjö](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%A4xj%C3%B6).[^cite_note-\:8-462] Celebrations of the attack were widely condemned by politicians, including by Prime Minister [Ulf Kristersson](3246360.md) and Opposition Leader [Magdalena Andersson](41178429.md).[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465]



Pro-Palestinian protest in Stockholm, Sweden, 2 November 2024

On 7 January 2024, protesters demonstrated against the war outside the US Embassy in [Stockholm](26741.md).[^cite_note-466] On 28 January, pro-Palestinian protests were held in Helsingborg, Malmö, and [Gothenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothenburg).[^cite_note-467] On 29 February, Swedish MEP [Abir Al-Sahlani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abir_Al-Sahlani) used her allotted speaking time during the parliamentary session to stage a silent protest.[^cite_note-468] On 27 April 2024, a protest took place in Sweden with chants of "Free Palestine" and "Boycott Israel".[^cite_note-AJE_proPalestine_second_week-325]

As the host of [Eurovision 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_2024), Malmö saw demonstrations because of [Israel's participation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest_2024) in the contest. On 9 May, roughly a hundred demonstrators gathered in Malmö for a pro-Israel rally while thousands had demonstrated against Israel's participation.[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470] On 11 May, thousands protested Eurovision in Malmö. Swedish climate activist [Greta Thunberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greta_Thunberg) was arrested protesting outside.[^cite_note-471] Israeli singer [Eden Golan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eden_Golan) was booed by the audience at a rehearsal.[^cite_note-472] A presenter from Norway withdrew after making public allegations of genocide against Israel. Several other performers missed rehearsals, complained about double standards committed by the [European Broadcasting Union](10380.md) and wore costumes and makeup with political messages that were explicitly forbidden by the rules of the contest.[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474]

&#x20;On 7 June, police in Stockholm detained 19 pro-Palestinian activists who had barricaded themselves inside a building of the [Royal Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Institute_of_Technology) while calling for the institution to end collaboration with Israeli universities.[^cite_note-475] In September 2024, Greta Thunberg was arrested while protesting for [Stockholm University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockholm_University) to cut its ties with Israel.[^cite_note-476]



Pro-Palestine protest in Geneva in front of the UN Office, 25 November 2023

### Switzerland

Main article: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_Switzerland)

On 25 November 2023, pro-Palestinian demonstrators gathered in front of the [United Nations Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Office_at_Geneva) in [Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva).[^cite_note-aljazeera-207]

On 13 January 2024, over 15,000 protesters supporting the Palestinian cause gathered in [Basel](4911.md) to stage a demonstration in solidarity with the people of Gaza. This gathering followed a prohibition imposed by several German-speaking Swiss [cantons](180752.md) on similar activities.[^cite_note-477]

On 2 May, hundreds of pro-Palestine students at the [University of Lausanne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Lausanne) staged a sit-in at a university facility, chanting "Gaza, UNIL is with you" and "down with Israeli apartheid".[^cite_note-478]



Pro-Palestinian protest during the opening ceremony of the Eurovision Song Contest in [Basel](4911.md), 11 May 2025

On 12 May, pro-Palestinian students demonstrated at the [University of Bern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Bern), occupying a university building.[^cite_note-479]

Israel's participation in the [Eurovision Song Contest 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2025) in Switzerland has sparked controversy.[^cite_note-480] The "Turquoise Carpet" event, held on 11 May 2025 in the host city Basel, was targeted by pro-Palestinian protesters against Israel's participation.[^cite_note-481]

### Ukraine

Many Ukrainians have supported Israel, viewing the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) as similar to the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-482] Ukraine's ambassador to Israel [Mykhailo Brodskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Brodskyy) described Ukraine as the most pro-Israel country in Europe, and said that rallies and gatherings in support of Israel occurred in the country despite prohibitions on gatherings in Ukraine due to the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md), such as in [Kyiv](585629.md), [Lviv](49968.md), [Odesa](55545.md), [Kharkiv](2788558.md), and [Dnipro](989795.md).[^cite_note-483]

On 2 November, the [left-wing](18499.md) magazine [*Commons*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commons_\(magazine\)) published an open letter from "Ukrainian researchers, artists, political and labour activists, members of civil society" expressing solidarity with Palestinians, and condemned Israel's actions in Gaza, Islamophobia and antisemitism. As of 27 December 2023, the letter has 443 signatures.[^cite_note-484]

According to Ukrainian researcher, Yuliia Kishchuk, she stated that many Ukrainians have gradually shifted their opinion of Israel's actions in Gaza, with some of them drew parallels with the [Holodomor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holodomor), which was considered as an act of genocide by Ukraine.[^cite_note-485]

### United Kingdom

Main article: [Gaza war protests in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_the_United_Kingdom)

See also: [List of pro-Palestinian protests in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_in_the_United_Kingdom)



Solidarity protest opposing the bombing of Gaza, [Cardiff](5882.md), 21 October 2023

In the weeks during Israel's counterattacks on Gaza, a number of pro-Palestine vigils, rallies and marches were held throughout the UK,[^cite_note-UKPPP14Oct-486][^cite_note-487] with the largest demonstrations held in [London](17867.md).[^cite_note-APNewPeacefully-488] At least 300,000 protestors attended a pro-Palestinian march on 11 November, which made it one of the largest pro-Palestinian protests in the Western world since 7 October.[^cite_note-APNewPeacefully-488] As well as London, rallies took place in [Manchester](20206.md) in northern England, [Edinburgh](9602.md) and [Glasgow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glasgow) in Scotland.[^cite_note-GalleryAlJazeera20231015-129]



Competing pro-Israel and pro-Palestine protests separated by police on a bridge in London, 4 July 2024.

Lawyers expressed concerns over war crimes committed by Israel,[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-Sherwood-490] and trade unions joined the calls for a ceasefire[^cite_note-GFTU-491][^cite_note-IWGB-492][^cite_note-493] — as did charities,[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495] cultural figures[^cite_note-496][^cite_note-497] and religious leaders.[^cite_note-498][^cite_note-499]

Companies that supply arms to Israel were targeted,[^cite_note-500][^cite_note-SimoneDailyEcho20231116-501][^cite_note-502] along with other companies viewed as supporting Israel,[^cite_note-503][^cite_note-504] with boycotts, protests and blockades.

The official position of both the UK's main political parties, the ruling [Conservatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) and the opposition [Labour](19279158.md), were supportive of Israel,[^cite_note-505][^cite_note-506] although there was dissent from politicians of both parties,[^cite_note-councillorletter-507][^cite_note-508] which was often met with punishment from party leaders.[^cite_note-509][^cite_note-510] A number of Labour politicians resigned from their roles or the party entirely in disagreement at the party's position.[^cite_note-511][^cite_note-512] Protests were directed at politicians,[^cite_note-513][^cite_note-514] particularly after a majority of Labour MPs abstained on a vote calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-camdenmarch-515][^cite_note-516][^cite_note-517]



Protest at [Lockheed Martin UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin) in [Havant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Havant), England, 17 June 2025

On 26 November, 60,000 marched in London to protest against a rise in antisemitic hate crimes from the war, making it the biggest protest against antisemitism since 1936.[^cite_note-518][^cite_note-519] In January 2024, thousands attended a pro-Israel rally in London to call for the release of all hostages from Gaza.[^cite_note-520] In March 2024, an estimated 400,000 people in London attended the tenth national march for Palestine since the war began.[^cite_note-521] In May 2024, pro-Palestinian marches were staged in Central London to mark the 76th anniversary of the Nakba.[^cite_note-522]

On 17 May 2025, approximately 600,000 people marched through London to commemorate the 77th anniversary of the Nakba and calling for the government to 'take action' to stop the atrocities against Palestinians.[^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524] On 4 June, thousands of people joined a pro-Palestine march outside of the [Parliament of the United Kingdom](13964.md) while wearing red to create a "Red Line for Palestine", demanded that the government impose an arms embargo and sanctions against Israel.[^cite_note-525]



Metropolitan Police making arrests at a [Palestine Action](67777543.md) protest in London, 6 September 2025

On 20 June 2025, pro-Palestinian activists led by [Palestine Action](67777543.md) managed to enter the [Brize Norton Royal Air Force base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAF_Brize_Norton) in [Oxfordshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfordshire). They damaged two Voyager aircraft with crowbars and used a repurposed fire extinguisher to spray red paint to the planes and the runway before being detained in the base.[^cite_note-526]

On 9 August 2025, hundreds of people gathered in [Parliament Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_Square) to protest against the ongoing genocide in Gaza and in opposition to the banning of Palestine Action, the [Metropolitan Police](192450.md) stated on X that they have arrested over 446 people in the square, with campaigners calling it the "largest ever mass arrest at a single protest in the British capital". Five officers were injured during the arrests.[^cite_note-527]

[A hunger strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Prisoners_for_Palestine_hunger_strike) by eight people imprisoned for activity related to Palestine Action began in November 2025, the largest hunger strike in the UK since the [1981 Irish hunger strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981_Irish_hunger_strike). It led to the hospitalisation of several prisoners.[^cite_note-528][^cite_note-529]

### Vatican City

On 25 December 2024, protesters waved Palestinian flags in the courtyard of [St. Peter's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Basilica) where [Pope Francis](1687680.md) lamented both the 7 October attack by Hamas and the "appalling harvest of innocent civilians" by Israel and called for a ceasefire during his Christmas Day [homily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homily).[^cite_note-530]

## Middle East

### Bahrain

Worshipers at the [Duraz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duraz) mosque chanted "[Death to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calls_for_the_destruction_of_Israel)" and "[Death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America)" during Friday prayers. A march followed, with participants shouting anti-Israel slogans including "No to [normalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain%E2%80%93Israel_normalization_agreement)!"[^cite_note-\:3-150] Bahraini officials detained numerous individuals involved in demonstrations supporting Palestine, including both minors and individuals who actively participated in advocating for Palestine online.[^cite_note-531]

In September 2025, protests and clashes erupted between pro-Palestine demonstrators and police in [Manama](20481.md) after the appointment of [Shmuel Revel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shmuel_\(Sammy\)_Revel) as the new Israeli ambassador of Bahrain after his predecessor, [Eitan Na'eh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eitan_Na'eh), left the country in November 2023.[^cite_note-532]

### Egypt

The Egyptian government, led by [Abdel-Fattah el-Sissi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdel-Fattah_el-Sissi), currently maintains diplomatic relations with both Israel and Hamas. It also banned large protests in 2013. Despite this, the majority of Egyptians support the Palestinian people and their aspirations for self-determination. Following Israel's bombardment of the Gaza Strip, the state authorized 27 sites for demonstrations in major cities across the country.[^cite_note-apnews.com-132]

On 11 October, it was reported that protesters chanted pro-Palestinian slogans and burned Israeli flags outside the Syndicate of Journalists' headquarters in [Cairo](6293.md).[^cite_note-533] On 13 October, protestors gathered at [Al-Azhar Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Azhar_Mosque) after Friday prayers and chanted for the [Al-Aqsa mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa_mosque).[^cite_note-ap-534]

On 20 October, tens of thousands demonstrated in cities across the country.[^cite_note-\:3-150] In Cairo, 43 people were reportedly arrested in an unauthorized pro-Palestine rally in [Tahrir Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahrir_Square).[^cite_note-535] There were also protests on the [Egypt–Gaza border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Gaza_border) near [Rafah](584373.md).[^cite_note-536]

On 15 January 2024, journalists gathered for "100 Days of Aggression: Stop the War, Prosecute War Criminals" at the Cairo headquarters of the journalists syndicate.[^cite_note-Mansour_2024-537][^cite_note-538] They called for Israel to stop killing Palestinians and journalists, condemned Israeli leaders as war criminals, and called for unhindered access to aid in Gaza. They also accused the Egyptian government of complicity in the suffering of Gaza.[^cite_note-539][^cite_note-Mansour_2024-537]

### Iran



Protest opposing Israeli policy in Gaza, 14 October, [Tehran](57654.md)



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Isfahan](223149.md), [Iran](14653.md)

Multiple rallies were held supporting Hamas and against the Israeli bombing of Gaza. Many protesters flew Palestinian flags and ones for [Hezbollah](13919.md), chanting anti-Israeli slogans. State media presented those rallies as "screams of a common pain...the pain of the Zionist regime's trampling on humanity".[^cite_note-\:0-172] During a football match in [Azadi Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azadi_Stadium) in [Tehran](57654.md), counter-protesters chanted an anti-Palestinian slogan.[^cite_note-540] Counter-protesters also chanted anti-Palestinian slogans at the funeral of murdered filmmaker [Dariush Mehrjui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dariush_Mehrjui).[^cite_note-541] Counter-protesters also created the "#IraniansStandWithIsrael" hashtag and hung Israeli flags.[^cite_note-542]

Protesters gathered in Tehran and other cities to protest against Israel's bombardment of Gaza and express solidarity with Gaza.[^cite_note-543] Likewise, "Death to Israel", Death to America", and "End the Genocide" and so forth.[^cite_note-544]

On 31 July 2024, protests were held in Tehran after the assassination of Hamas leader Ismail Haniyeh.[^cite_note-GalleryAlJazeera20240801-153]

### Iraq

On 13 October, thousands gathered in [Liberation Square, Baghdad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Square,_Baghdad), waving Palestinian flags and burning Israeli flags while chanting anti-Israeli and anti-American slogans.[^cite_note-\:0-172] Demonstrators also gathered at a bridge leading to the [Green Zone](856542.md), while others held a sit-in at the [Trebil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trebil) border crossing with Jordan. Demonstrators in [Baghdad](4492.md) vowed to keep up their protest "until the roads are opened in order to send aid" to Gaza.[^cite_note-\:3-150] Protesters marched in Baghdad in February 2024, holding signs, such as one reading: "The starvation of Gaza's children is a shame for all humanity."[^cite_note-545] The largest protest was held in the streets of Baghdad.[^cite_note-protesters13october-546]

On 31 July 2024, a march was held in Baghdad to condemn the assassination of Ismail Haniyeh.[^cite_note-GalleryAlJazeera20240801-153]

On 19 October, dozens of people stormed and looted the headquarters of the Saudi-based news channel [MBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBC_Group) in Baghdad after it referred to the insurgents as "terrorists", including [Hamas](13913.md), [Hezbollah](13919.md), and the [Popular Mobilization Forces](45671374.md).[^cite_note-547]

### Jordan

Thousands participated in a march on 1 December to demonstrate solidarity with the Palestinians in the Gaza Strip. The procession commenced following Friday prayers in the vicinity of the [Grand Husseini Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Husseini_Mosque) in Amman.[^cite_note-Thousands_march_for_Palestine-548] Riot police fired tear gas to disperse 500 pro-Palestinian protesters at a security checkpoint outside of [Amman](45007.md) on 13 October, as they were heading to the border with the [West Bank](33209.md). Protests also emerged in [Irbid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irbid) and [Zarqa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zarqa), while anti-Israel marches were denied at the border areas in the [Jordan Valley](12105764.md), according to the [Ministry of Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_\(Jordan\)).[^cite_note-JordanDispersesProtestersReuters-549]

Protests were held following the end of the ceasefire in Gaza on 1 December.[^cite_note-Thousands_march_for_Palestine-548] On 8 December, protesters demonstrated outside the U.S. Embassy in Amman, calling for a boycott of U.S. products.[^cite_note-550]

On 23 January 2024 protests were held at the Egyptian embassy in Amman against the blockade of Gaza.[^cite_note-551] On 6 February, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) reported that hundreds of pro-Palestinian activists had been arrested by Jordanian authorities.[^cite_note-552] On 27 March, police used tear gas and batons on protesters attempting to storm the Israeli embassy.[^cite_note-553] On 23 April, [Al-Qassam Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izz_ad-Din_al-Qassam_Brigades) spokesman [Abu Obaida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Obaida) called on the "Jordanian masses" to escalate their actions and "raise their voice" against Israel.[^cite_note-554]

On 6 July demonstrations were held in Amman in solidarity with Gaza.[^cite_note-555] On 14 July protests were held near the Israeli embassy in Amman in solidarity with Gaza which took place a day after the [attacks in al-Mawasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/13_July_2024_Al-Mawasi_airstrikes).[^cite_note-556] On 2 August demonstrations broke out near the Israeli embassy denouncing the assassination of Ismail Haniyeh.[^cite_note-557]

### Kuwait

On 14 January 2024, hundreds of protestors held a demonstration in [Al-Erada Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Erada_Square), [Kuwait City](57021.md) to show solidarity with Palestinians and marking the 100th day since the 7 October attacks on Israel.[^cite_note-558]

### Lebanon

Demonstrations broke out across the country on 13 October, with protestors flying [Hezbollah](13919.md), [LCP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Communist_Party) and [SSNP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Social_Nationalist_Party_in_Lebanon) flags while chanting pro-Palestinian slogans.[^cite_note-ap-534] Following [the explosion at al-Ahli Arab Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion), demonstrators gathered at the [US](73803702.md) and [French embassies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_France,_Beirut) in [Beirut](37428.md) over their countries' support for Israel.[^cite_note-559] Demonstrators gathered outside the South African embassy to thank them for bringing Israel to the International Court of Justice.[^cite_note-560] Demonstrators protested at UN headquarters in Beirut in support of [UNRWA](32270.md).[^cite_note-561]

On 30 April, inspired by the protests in American universities, over 200 people gathered around the campus ground at the [American University of Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University_of_Beirut), the gatherers had the administration's permission to protest for two hours as the protests calls for the [boycott of Israeli businesses](43473050.md).[^cite_note-562]

On 15 May a pro-Palestinian demonstration was staged in Beirut during the 76th anniversary of the [Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba), the demonstration started at the American University of Beirut and ended at the British Embassy.[^cite_note-563]

On 31 July 2024, demonstrations were held in [Sidon](28366.md) to condemn the assassination of Ismail Haniyeh with masked boys holding replicas of [assault rifles](18950865.md).[^cite_note-GalleryAlJazeera20240801-153]

### Oman

On 19 October 2023, citizens and expats gathered to protest against Israel's war on Gaza.[^cite_note-564] Meanwhile, US diplomats indicated that there were calls for protests in [Muscat](57053.md) on 19 and 20 October.[^cite_note-565]

### Qatar

On 16 December thousands gathered at the [Education City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_City_Stadium) in [Doha](26214389.md) to show their support and help raise money for Palestinians.[^cite_note-566]

### Syria

On 13 October, protesters in [Damascus](8914.md) including Palestinians from the [Yarmouk camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yarmouk_camp) rallied for the people in Gaza.[^cite_note-ap-534] On 27 October, a march was organised in [Jaramana Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaramana_Camp) to show solidarity with Palestinians in Gaza.[^cite_note-567]

In October 2025, hundreds of Syrians from several cities in the country including [Aleppo](159244.md), [Deir ez-Zor](1429146.md), and [Hama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hama) gathered to express solidarity with Palestinians and condemned Israel's war on Gaza.[^cite_note-568]

### Turkey

On 13 October, thousands of demonstrators crowded at [Beyazit Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyazit_Square) in [Istanbul](3391396.md) to show solidarity with Palestinians.[^cite_note-569] Protests erupted in [Ankara](802.md) after the [al-Ahli Arab Hospital explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion).[^cite_note-570] The Israeli Consulate experienced a forceful intrusion by protesters who directed fireworks toward the premises and ignited Israeli flags. Moreover, a group of demonstrators made an effort to enter an American military base. In Istanbul, protesters gathered outside the US consulate to demand its immediate closure.[^cite_note-571] Demonstrators linked to Islamic organizations in Istanbul displayed [Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Turkey) and Palestinian flags, raised signs, and vocalized slogans condemning Israel's activities in Gaza.[^cite_note-apnews.com-132]

On 5 November, police in [Adana Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adana_Province) fired water cannon and tear gas to disperse a large crowd of protesters as they attempted to storm [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md) which housed US troops.[^cite_note-572] On 6 November, protesters in Ankara demonstrated against U.S. Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken)'s visit, holding signs that read "murderer Blinken, get out of Turkey!" and "No to genocide!"[^cite_note-573] On 31 December, protesters demonstrated outside the US embassy in Istanbul.[^cite_note-574]

In February 2024, a gunman occupied a [Procter & Gamble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procter_&_Gamble) factory in [Gebze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gebze), holding seven workers there hostage in protest of the [Israeli invasion of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_invasion_of_the_Gaza_Strip_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)).[^cite_note-theguardian.com-575] After he left the factory to use the bathroom, he was arrested and the hostages were freed with no one, including the gunman, suffering any injuries.[^cite_note-theguardian.com-575]

On 7 April 2024, a group by the name of "A Thousand Youths for Palestine Initiative" organised protests in [Beyoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyo%C4%9Flu), [Istanbul](3391396.md) against [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) and his [Justice and Development Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_and_Development_Party_\(Turkey\)) due to their continued trade and relations with Israel despite making many statements in support of Palestine, in which Turkish trade with Israel had actually increased after the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-\:10-576] A month earlier, the group had also disrupted an Erdoğan rally in [Ankara](802.md) with the same demands.[^cite_note-577] The protestors gathered on [İstiklal Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0stiklal_Avenue), near [Galatasaray High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galatasaray_High_School), where police quickly attempted to disperse the protest, in which 43 people were detained, and 5 of them were kept and tried.[^cite_note-578] Heavy [police brutality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_brutality) was reported, with the protestors being assaulted and extreme measures taken to quell the protests.[^cite_note-\:10-576][^cite_note-579]

On 3 May 2024, a pro-Palestine protest at [Gazi University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gazi_University) in [Ankara](802.md) turned into a riot between Palestine supporters and Turkish nationalists. Students gathered to pray [Friday prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friday_prayer) together and set up rallies as part of the [2024 pro-Palestinian university protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses). Turkish and Palestinian flags were flown. Many professors and other students attended the rally, where speeches in Turkish, Arabic, and English were made.[^cite_note-580] A group of Turkish nationalists had also hosted a rally nearby commemorating [Turkism Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkism_Day). The pro-Palestinians later began shouting slogans in Arabic, and [takbirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takbir), which provoked the Turkish nationalists into intruding the university and assaulting the students while shouting "[how happy is the one who says I am a Turk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/How_happy_is_the_one_who_says_I_am_a_Turk)".[^cite_note-habervakti.com-581][^cite_note-irkcilar-gazze-eyleminden-rahatsiz-oldu-582] Security had to intervene and escort the pro-Palestinians to safety inside the university.[^cite_note-583] Most of the attackers were described as [Victory Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Party_\(Turkey\)) members and other [Kemalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kemalism).[^cite_note-584][^cite_note-irkcilar-gazze-eyleminden-rahatsiz-oldu-582] Some [MHP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Movement_Party) [Grey Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_Wolves_\(organization\)) and other [Idealists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idealism_\(Turkey\)) were present, where they gave the university students a chance to stop the Arabic chanting, before joining the assaults after the students refused.[^cite_note-585] The attackers referred to themselves as "Turkists" and "nationalists", and did not specify any political affiliation.[^cite_note-586] After the attacks, members of the "Ankara Palestine Solidarity Platform" protested outside the university and demanded justice.[^cite_note-habervakti.com-581]



Protest for Gaza in Istanbul, 13 April 2025

On 6 May 2024, protests were held in Istanbul in demanding the end of the war and a ceasefire.[^cite_note-587]

On 26 May, protesters threw [molotov cocktails](20922.md) at the Israeli consulate in Istanbul following the [Tel al-Sultan massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_al-Sultan_massacre). Demonstrations also occurred in front of the home of the [US Consul General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consulate_General_of_the_United_States,_Istanbul) and at Saraçhane Park.[^cite_note-588]

On 31 July 2024, protests were held in Istanbul against the assassination of Ismail Haniyeh.[^cite_note-GalleryAlJazeera20240801-153]

On New Year's Day 2025, tens of thousands of people marched on [Galata Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galata_Bridge) in Istanbul to express solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-589]

On 13 and 20 April 2025, thousands of people across Turkey protested against Israel's attacks against civilians in the Gaza Strip under the motto, "Gaza is dying! Stand up!"[^cite_note-590]

On 1 January 2026, thousands of protesters started the new year again by marching across Galata Bridge in Istanbul in support of Gaza. It was reported that over 500,000 people attended the protest organized by over 400 civil society organizations.[^cite_note-591][^cite_note-592]

### United Arab Emirates

In December 2023, more than 100 people gathered at the [2023 United Nations Climate Change Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference) in [Dubai](211583.md) to call for a ceasefire in the Gaza war and expressed solidarity with the Palestinians.[^cite_note-593]

### Yemen



Protests in [Sana'a](18691665.md), Yemen following the [2024 missile strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_missile_strikes_in_Yemen)

In [Houthi](17240332.md)-controlled [Sanaa](18691665.md), demonstrators waved both [Yemeni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Yemen) and Palestinian flags and chanted "[Death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America)" and "[Death to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calls_for_the_destruction_of_Israel);"[^cite_note-ap-534] and tens of thousands of people took part in the march to show solidarity with Palestinians.[^cite_note-Thousands_march_for_Palestine-548] On 12 January 2024, tens of thousands protested at Sab'een Square in Sanaa against the US-UK bombings.[^cite_note-594] College students in Sanaa protested against US-UK Bombings on 18 January.[^cite_note-595]

Protesters on 19 January 2024 reportedly stated, "We don't care about whatever you do to us, we will continue our support and resilience with Palestinians until Israel stops its war on Palestine".[^cite_note-596][^cite_note-597] On 28 June 2024, protests were held in Sana'a with people carrying banners that said "There is no dignity for nations without victory for Gaza", smaller demonstrations were held in other governorates, including [Hodeidah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Hudaydah_Governorate), [Hajjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hajjah_Governorate), [Saada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saada_Governorate), [Taiz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiz_governorate), and [Ad Dali](3604352.md).[^cite_note-aje.io-131] In Sanaa, thousands of protesters waved Palestinian flags, and chanted: "With our souls, with our blood, we sacrifice for you ... oh Palestinians."[^cite_note-apnews.com-132]

On 3 August 2024, thousands participated in a "Day of Rage" by protesting in Sanaa, mourning the assassination of Ismail Haniyeh.[^cite_note-598]

## North America

### Barbados

In November 2023, a pro-Palestinian march took place in [Bridgetown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridgetown), involving more than 100 people, with protesters chanting, "Palestine will be free".[^cite_note-599]

### Belize

On 22 November 2023, a pro-Palestine march took place in [Belize City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize_City) with about 150 people attending. The march was also attended by Prime Minister [Johnny Briceño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnny_Brice%C3%B1o), Deputy Prime Minister [Cordel Hyde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordel_Hyde), Minister of Home Affairs [Kareem Musa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kareem_Musa), and several other politicians.[^cite_note-600]

### Canada



28 October rally in support of Palestine in Toronto, Canada

On 10 October, a large pro-Israel rally took place in [Toronto](64646.md), which attracted a crowd of 15,000 people, including MP [Melissa Lantsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melissa_Lantsman) and Mayor [Olivia Chow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivia_Chow).[^cite_note-601] Pro-Palestinian rallies occurred in major cities including [Ottawa](22219.md), [Montreal](7954681.md), [Edmonton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmonton), [Calgary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calgary), [Windsor](33596.md) and [Vancouver](32706.md).[^cite_note-602][^cite_note-603][^cite_note-604] On 30 October, [Independent Jewish Voices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Jewish_Voices_Canada) staged [sit-ins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in) demanding a ceasefire at 17 Canadian politicians' offices, including that of Foreign Minister [Melanie Joly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melanie_Joly).[^cite_note-605][^cite_note-606] On 12 November, a pro-Israel rally in Toronto attracted a crowd of 5,000.[^cite_note-607]

On 15 November, protestors heckled Prime Minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) at a restaurant in Vancouver: "You have blood on your hands."[^cite_note-608] Protestors in [St. John's](70581.md) also called for a ceasefire during a summit held there between Trudeau and [EU Commission President](24468.md) [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md).[^cite_note-609][^cite_note-610] The [Saskatchewan Legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saskatchewan_Legislature) was disrupted by protesters calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-611] Around 20,000 pro-Israel protesters held a rally in Ottawa's [Parliament Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_Hill) on 4 December.[^cite_note-612] [Ontario](22218.md) Premier [Doug Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doug_Ford) called pro-Palestinian protests in the province "hate rallies".[^cite_note-613] The [National Council of Canadian Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Council_of_Canadian_Muslims) announced on 29 January 2024 it would refuse a meeting with Trudeau over his government's failure to urge Israel to follow the [International Court of Justice](14918.md)'s order to not commit genocide.[^cite_note-614]



Pro-Palestinian protest in Toronto, 4 November 2023

On 5 March, a man in Toronto was arrested after attacking pro-Palestinian protesters outside of a synagogue using a [nail gun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nail_gun) while shouting "every Palestinian will die".[^cite_note-615]

On 7 March, a court in [Quebec](7954867.md) issued a temporary ban on pro-Palestinian protests within 50 metres of a synagogue and four other Jewish community buildings in [Montreal](7954681.md).[^cite_note-616]

On 14 April, protestors in Toronto cheered an [aerial attack launched by Iran on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_against_Israel), chanting "[Allahu Akbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takbir)!" A protest leader characterized the strikes as a response to Israeli airstrikes in the region, while [Melissa Lantsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melissa_Lantsman), a Canadian member of parliament, said that the chants showed "It was never about a ceasefire". Other chants included "Intifada, Intifada, long live the Intifada" and "From the water to the water, Palestine is Arab".[^cite_note-617]

On 9 June, Canada's *[National Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Post)* said that over 50,000 pro-Israel supporters were believed to have marched in Toronto's annual [Walk With Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walk_With_Israel).[^cite_note-618]

On 6 October 2024, rallies broke out in Toronto in solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-ReferenceD-619]

On 14 June 2025, thousands of people joined a pro-Palestine march in Montreal to denounce the humanitarian crisis in Gaza and calling on the government to pressure Israel to reach a ceasefire.[^cite_note-620]

#### Universities

A dense group of tents on a university lawn in downtown Montreal, surrounded by fences bearing banners

Approximately 80 tents form the [encampment at McGill University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McGill_University_pro-Palestinian_encampment), Montreal, 2 May 2024

See also: [McGill University pro-Palestinian encampment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McGill_University_pro-Palestinian_encampment)

Further information: [2024 pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses)

This section is an excerpt from [List of Gaza war protests at universities in 2024 § Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_in_2024#Canada).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=List_of_Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_in_2024\&action=edit)]

On April 22, students set up an encampment at the [University of Alberta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Alberta), in [Edmonton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmonton).[^cite_note-621] A week later, on April 29, an encampment of about 30 tents was set up on the Point Grey campus of the [University of British Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_British_Columbia), in [Vancouver](32706.md).[^cite_note-List_of_Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_in_2024_Ip-2024-622] About 100 protesters were present on its first day, calling on the institution to divest from Israel.[^cite_note-623][^cite_note-List_of_Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_in_2024_Ip-2024-622]



[University of Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto) encampment as of May 20, 2024



University of Toronto encampement on May 26, 2024

On May 1, an encampment was built outside Tabaret Hall at the [University of Ottawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Ottawa).[^cite_note-624] On [Vancouver Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver_Island), encampments were set up at the [University of Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Victoria) and [Vancouver Island University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver_Island_University);[^cite_note-625] and an encampment was also briefly set up at the [University of Western Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Western_Ontario), lasting a day before disappearing.[^cite_note-626] The following day, students at the [University of Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto) set up an encampment of about 50 tents at King's College Circle on its [St. George campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto_St._George).[^cite_note-627] On May 5, an encampment was set up at [McMaster University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McMaster_University),[^cite_note-628] and a protest was held at the [University of Waterloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Waterloo) the day after.[^cite_note-629] On May 7, an encampment was set up at the [University of Manitoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Manitoba), scheduled to last for three days.[^cite_note-630] Protests were held at [Trent University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trent_University) and [Emily Carr University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Carr_University) the following day.[^cite_note-631][^cite_note-632] On May 9, an encampment was set up at the [University of Windsor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Windsor),[^cite_note-633] and police in [Calgary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calgary) used flashbangs to remove protesters from an encampment set up at the [University of Calgary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Calgary).[^cite_note-634] The next day, a die-in protest was held at [Memorial University of Newfoundland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_University_of_Newfoundland).[^cite_note-635]

On May 12, an encampment was set up at [Dalhousie University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalhousie_University),[^cite_note-636] an encampment was set up at the University of Waterloo the following day.[^cite_note-637] An encampment was set up at [UBC Okanagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_British_Columbia_Okanagan) on May 14,[^cite_note-638] and protests were also held at the [University of Saskatchewan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Saskatchewan) and [University of Regina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Regina) on May 17.[^cite_note-639][^cite_note-640] On May 21, an encampment was set up at the [University of Guelph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Guelph),[^cite_note-641] and students attending [Memorial University of Newfoundland and Labrador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_University_of_Newfoundland_and_Labrador) set up an encampment outside the Art's and Administration Building in [St. John's](70581.md).[^cite_note-642]

On May 23, students at [Simon Fraser University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Fraser_University) occupied their campus' Belzberg Library to demand divestment from military assets supplied to Israel, coinciding with a Board of Governors meeting. Despite protests and an open letter, the Board did not discuss divestment.[^cite_note-643] On May 30, SFU president [Joy Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joy_Johnson_\(university_administrator\)) and the Board of Governors agreed to discuss divestment from arms companies after community pressure, protests, and a faculty vote. They committed to reviewing their Responsible Investment Policy with community input.[^cite_note-644]

On June 3, protesters occupied a [Concordia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concordia_University) building for around an hour.[^cite_note-645] On June 5, an encampment was set up at [York University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/York_University),[^cite_note-646] which was subsequently cleared by [Toronto police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_Police_Service) on June 6.[^cite_note-647]

On June 7, University of Waterloo agreed to several demands to disclose investments and to base investment decisions on [human rights](13831.md) and other social factors.[^cite_note-648]

On June 10, a protest was held at [Brock University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brock_University).[^cite_note-649]

On October 7, 2024, walkouts were held on all three of the [University of Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto)'s campuses in [downtown Toronto (St. George)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto_St._George), [Mississauga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto_Mississauga), and [Scarborough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto_Scarborough).[^cite_note-650]

### Cuba

On 23 November 2023, thousands marched in the [Paseo del Prado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paseo_del_Prado,_Havana) of [Havana](49719.md) in solidarity with the Palestinian cause, including President [Miguel Díaz-Canel](38630938.md), who wore a [keffiyeh](47446641.md) to the event, Prime Minister [Manuel Marrero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Marrero), Foreign Minister [Bruno Rodríguez Parrilla](15731273.md), and several Palestinian medical students who were in Cuba as part of a cooperation program. Prior to the demonstration, the colors of the Palestinian flag were projected on to the [José Martí Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mart%C3%AD_Memorial).[^cite_note-651]

On 14 October 2024, President Diaz-Canel organized a protest in Havana in solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-652]

On 9 October 2025, thousands of people gathered in front of the U.S. embassy to protest against the Gaza war and to show support to the Palestinians. The demonstration was organized by President Diaz-Canel and the government.[^cite_note-653]

### Dominican Republic

On 19 October 2023, about a hundred people gathered at Parque Independencia in [Santo Domingo](57638.md) to protest against Israel's actions towards the Palestinians and express solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-654]

### Jamaica

In May 2024, several protesters held a march in [Half Way Tree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Half_Way_Tree), [Saint Andrew Parish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Andrew_Parish,_Jamaica) in support of Palestine and demanding the Jamaican government to cut diplomatic ties with Israel.[^cite_note-655]

### Mexico



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Mexico City](18987.md), 29 November 2023



Pro-Palestinian protest in Mexico City, 17 August 2025

Over 3,000 rallied in solidarity with Israel in [Mexico City](18987.md) on 15 October.[^cite_note-656]

Palestine supporters gathered at the Israeli embassy in Mexico City.[^cite_note-aljazeera-207] On 5 November, thousands marched in Mexico City from the [Angel of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_of_Independence) monument to the [Zócalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z%C3%B3calo), chanting, "break, break [ties with Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Mexico_relations)" and "it is not a war, it is a genocide".[^cite_note-657] Anti-war protesters hit [piñatas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pi%C3%B1ata) with the faces of Joe Biden and Netanyahu at a protest on 8 January in Mexico City.[^cite_note-658]

On 13 January 2024, as a part of the "global day of action", thousands took to the streets of Mexico City in solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-659] On 18 February, hundreds of pro-Palestinian protesters took to Mexico City's [Paseo de la Reforma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paseo_de_la_Reforma) demanding a ceasefire.[^cite_note-660][^cite_note-661] On 28 May 2024, following the [Tel al-Sultan massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_al-Sultan_massacre), an estimated 200 people rallied in the [Lomas de Chapultepec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lomas_de_Chapultepec) neighborhood of Mexico City. Protests were held in front of the Israeli embassy and culminated in clashes with the [police](9964117.md).[^cite_note-662] On 29 May, protestors denouncing the Rafah offensive threw molotov cocktails into the embassy, causing minor damage. That same month, a solidarity encampment was set up at the [National Autonomous University of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Autonomous_University_of_Mexico) by students demanding the breaking of diplomatic relations with Israel.[^cite_note-663]

On 6 October 2024, demonstrations broke out in Mexico City, marching on the streets with Palestinian flags and banners urging President [Claudia Sheinbaum](57813311.md) to break off ties with Israel.[^cite_note-ReferenceD-619]

### Trinidad and Tobago

On 14 October 2023, about 300 members of the Muslim community gathered at [Queen's Park Savannah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen's_Park_Savannah) in [Port of Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Spain) to show solidarity with the Palestinians.[^cite_note-664]

### United States

Main article: [Gaza war protests in the United States](75347645.md)

See also: [List of U.S. officials who resigned over Biden's support for Israel in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._officials_who_resigned_over_Biden's_support_for_Israel_in_the_Gaza_war)



14 November, 2023 rally in support of Israel in Washington, D.C.

On 8 October 2023, one day after [Hamas'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_7_attack) attack on Israel, hundreds of pro-Palestinian demonstrators rallied in [New York City](645042.md)'s [Times Square](31389.md), waving Palestinian flags, chanting "Resistance is Justified", "[Globalize the Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalize_the_Intifada)", "[From the river to the sea, Palestine will be free](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea,_Palestine_will_be_free)", "By any means necessary", "Glory to our martyrs", and "Smash the settler Zionist state".[^cite_note-665][^cite_note-666][^cite_note-timesofisrael.com-667] Dozens of pro-Israeli protesters also organised a counter-protest, singing [Hatikvah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatikvah) and chanting "Never again" and "[The people of Israel live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Am_Yisrael_Chai)" in Hebrew.[^cite_note-timesofisrael.com-667]

In the ten days after the attack, the US held more than 400 related [protests](266137.md) and [vigils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vigils).[^cite_note-DCdemand-668] There were an estimated 180,000 demonstrators and protestors, with roughly even numbers attending events related to Israeli and Palestinian concerns.[^cite_note-DCdemand-668] US elected officials overwhelmingly attended more events in support of Israel. An estimated one-third of all anti-war protests were met with [counter-protesters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-protest), who at times were violent.[^cite_note-crowdcountUS-669] By the second week of the war, the number of pro-Palestinian protests in the US outnumbered the number of Pro-Israel protests.[^cite_note-670]

Students in the United States reported fears of losing future job opportunities for engaging in protests against military action in Gaza.[^cite_note-671] More than 260 "[incidents of suppression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Exception)" were reported against people opposed to the war.[^cite_note-672] [Palestine Legal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Legal), a non-profit representing people who lost their jobs for protesting for Palestinian rights, described the environment in the United States as "[McCarthyite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCarthyism)".[^cite_note-673] The [Israel on Campus Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_on_Campus_Coalition) reportedly engaged in covert espionage against pro-Palestinian student organizations.[^cite_note-674] [Accuracy in Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accuracy_in_Media) sent [doxxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxxing) trucks to college campuses around the United States, displaying the names and faces of people it perceives to be pro-Palestinian.[^cite_note-\:6-675][^cite_note-676] [Canary Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canary_Mission) produced [blacklists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacklist) of students involved with pro-Palestinian activism.[^cite_note-677] The [ACLU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACLU) urged US colleges to protect the free speech of pro-Palestinian and anti-war protestors.[^cite_note-678] The [National Lawyers Guild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Lawyers_Guild) condemned all attacks on pro-Palestinian activism at universities.[^cite_note-679]



[March on Washington for Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_on_Washington_for_Gaza) in January 2024

[Congressional staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_staff) for Democrat representatives reported receiving an overwhelming amount of calls and emails from constituents demanding their representatives to support a ceasefire. One staffer noted: "The phone doesn't stop ringing at any point in the day."[^cite_note-680] On 4 November 2023, the [National March on Washington: Free Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_March_on_Washington:_Free_Palestine) was held at the [National Mall](167796.md).[^cite_note-681] At least two Americans — a woman in Atlanta and [Aaron Bushnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-immolation_of_Aaron_Bushnell) in Washington, D.C. — [self-immolated](916669.md) in protest of [U.S. policy in Gaza](65284610.md), the latter of whom later died.[^cite_note-682][^cite_note-683] The identity of the woman who self-immolated was not publicly released, but she was reported to be in critical condition as of 2 December 2023. Police described it as an "act of extreme political protest", assuring the public that there was no terrorist threat to the Israeli consulate.[^cite_note-684] Israeli consul general [Anat Sultan-Dadon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anat_Sultan-Dadon?action=edit\&redlink=1) said it was "tragic to see the hate and incitement toward Israel expressed in such a horrific way."[^cite_note-685][^cite_note-686] Bushnell, an active duty member of the [United States Air Force](32090.md), shouted "Free Palestine" as he burned.[^cite_note-687] He was taken to a hospital where he later died.[^cite_note-688] [Tax Day protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_Day_protests) were held in several major cities on 15 April 2024.[^cite_note-689]

#### Puerto Rico

Hundreds of Puerto Ricans held pro-Palestinian demonstrations in [San Juan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Juan,_Puerto_Rico) denouncing Israel in October[^cite_note-EFEPR-690] and November 2023.[^cite_note-691][^cite_note-692] When US vice president [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) visited the island on 22 March 2024, several dozen people from [Puerto Rican independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Rican_independence) groups organised a pro-Palestinian protest criticising Israel and the United States.[^cite_note-693][^cite_note-694] Many Puerto Rican activists see similarities between Palestinians and themselves relating to struggles against perceived colonialism.[^cite_note-EFEPR-690][^cite_note-695]

#### US Virgin Islands

On 4 November 2023, members from the Arab community of [Saint Croix](229431.md) as well as some black, white and Hispanic supporters showed solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-696]
