## Oceania

### Australia

Main articles: [Gaza war protests in Australia](77971735.md) and [2025 Sydney Harbour Bridge protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Sydney_Harbour_Bridge_protest)

Further information: [List of pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses in 2024 § Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses_in_2024#Australia)



Protest opposing the bombing of Gaza, [Melbourne](17306237.md), Australia, 15 October

Following the outbreak of the Gaza war in October 2023, pro-Palestinian protests were held in several Australian cities including [Sydney](27862.md), [Melbourne](17306237.md), [Adelaide](1148.md), [Brisbane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brisbane) and Perth.[^cite_note-\:5-697][^cite_note-698][^cite_note-Guardian_12_Nov_2023-699][^cite_note-700] Protests at the [Sydney Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Opera_House) and [Lakemba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakemba,_New_South_Wales) were criticised by [New South Wales Premier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_of_New_South_Wales) [Chris Minns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Minns) and [Australian Prime Minister](24117.md) [Anthony Albanese](556745.md) for alleged antisemitic rhetoric and violence.[^cite_note-701][^cite_note-702]

Notable pro-Palestinian protests and campaigns have included weekly marches in Melbourne, Jewish peace activists occupying the electorate office of [Defence Minister](593152.md) [Richard Marles](4386805.md), blockades at the [Port of Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Melbourne) and [Port Botany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Botany), the establishment of [protest encampments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses#Australia) at several Australian universities, and the [Melbourne Land Forces Expo](77971735.md#2024_Melbourne_Land_Forces_Expo_protests) protests.[^cite_note-703][^cite_note-\:7-704][^cite_note-705][^cite_note-706][^cite_note-Cassidy-2024-707][^cite_note-708] Other notable protest actions have included picketing a Melbourne hotel hosting a delegation of Israeli kidnapping and murder victims and [Qantas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qantas) flight crew donning [Palestinian flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Palestine) badges.[^cite_note-709][^cite_note-710]

Pro-Israel counter-protests have also been held in Sydney and Melbourne.[^cite_note-711][^cite_note-712]

### Cook Islands

On 8 November, a small group of protesters gathered outside the National Auditorium in [Avarua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avarua) during the 2023 [Pacific Islands Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum) meeting calling for an end to the killing of Palestinian children in Gaza.[^cite_note-713][^cite_note-714]

### Fiji

On 2 June 2026, nearly a hundred people staged a pro-Palestinian demonstration at the Fiji Women's Crisis Centre in [Suva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suva) to protest against the opening of the Israeli embassy at the Fijian Holdings Limited Tower. The protest was staged by members of the Non-Government Organisation Coalition for Human Rights and Fijians for Palestine.[^cite_note-715] Israel's Foreign Minister [Gideon Sa'ar](485357.md) criticized the protest, stating that the demonstration was part of an agenda to "destroy the Jewish state".[^cite_note-716]

### New Zealand

Main article: [Gaza war protests in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_New_Zealand)



Palestine protest march from the US Embassy, Wellington in 2024

Since the outbreak of the [Gaza war](74998519.md) on 7 October 2023, both pro-Palestinian and pro-Israel protest events have been held across several [New Zealand](4913064.md) centres including [Auckland](18660332.md), [Wellington](33804.md), [Christchurch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christchurch), [Palmerston North](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmerston_North), [Nelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson,_New_Zealand) and [Dunedin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunedin).[^cite_note-The_Spinoff_25_Oct_2023-717][^cite_note-RNZ_28_Oct_2023-718] Pro-Palestinian protesters including the Palestine Solidarity Network Aotearoa New Zealand's leader [John Minto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Minto) have called for a ceasefire in Gaza and the suspension of diplomatic, economic and institutional relations and linkages between New Zealand and Israel.[^cite_note-NZH_21_Oct_2023-719][^cite_note-RNZ_23_Nov_2023-720][^cite_note-ODT_9_Oct_2024-721] Notable protest campaigns have included an attempted blockade of the [Ports of Auckland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ports_of_Auckland), protest events at several university campuses in mid-2024 and a controversial hotline targeting Israeli visitors of military age in January 2025.[^cite_note-RNZ_23_Nov_2023-720][^cite_note-The_Press_23_May_2024-722][^cite_note-NZH_28_Jan_2025-723]

Pro-Israel figures such as [Destiny Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destiny_Church_\(New_Zealand\)) leader [Brian Tamaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Tamaki) have organised pro-Israel counter-protests have been held across New Zealand. These have sought to express support for Israeli actions and to counter alleged [antisemitism](1078.md).[^cite_note-RNZ_7_Dec_2023-724][^cite_note-RNZ_11_Dec_2024-725]

### Samoa

In December 2023, a pro-Palestine group called Samoans for Ceasefire peacefully marched along Beach Road in the capital [Apia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apia), to rally for actions from the government to support and commit their efforts in calling for a ceasefire in Gaza. Later, in January 2024, a pro-Israel protest was orgasnised by the Samoa Prayer for Israel.[^cite_note-726][^cite_note-727]

## South America

### Argentina



A pro-Palestine march in [Buenos Aires](255919.md), 12 July 2025.

A pro-Israel rally was held in [Buenos Aires](255919.md) on 9 October that was attended by mayor [Horacio Rodríguez Larreta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horacio_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Larreta) and presidential candidate [Patricia Bullrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricia_Bullrich).[^cite_note-728] On 7 October 2024, thousands attended a rally in Buenos Aires, backed by President [Javier Milei](56599840.md), to mark the first anniversary of Hamas' attack on Israel.[^cite_note-729]

In September 2024, hundreds of Argentinians, including members from Arab and Palestinian communities, participated in a pro-Palestine rally in Buenos Aires, calling for the end of Israel's operations in Gaza.[^cite_note-730] On 15 June 2025, thousands marched in Buenos Aires in support of the Global March to Gaza.[^cite_note-731] On 12 July 2025, another pro-Palestine march took part in Buenos Aires and other parts of the country, known as the "red tide" because its protesters wore red garments and elements.[^cite_note-732]

### Brazil



Protest against the war in Gaza, [Recife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recife), Brazil, 19 October 2023

On 10 October, members of the [Palestinian community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Brazilian) protested outside the [National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Complex_of_the_Republic) in [Brasília](4752.md).[^cite_note-apnews-442] On 15 October, members of the Israeli community rallied in [Copacabana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copacabana,_Rio_de_Janeiro), [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md).[^cite_note-733] On 13 January 2024, a pro-Palestine march was held in [São Paulo](390875.md) calling for a ceasefire as part of the "Global day of action".[^cite_note-734]

In February 2024, thousands gathered for a pro-Israel rally held by former president [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md) to protest against President [Lula da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lula_da_Silva)'s remarks comparing Gaza to the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust).[^cite_note-735]

### Chile

A benefit concert was held at Padre Hurtado Park in [Santiago](51572.md) on 25 October to raise funds for hospitals in the Palestinian territories and express solidarity with civilians in Gaza. Among the performers was [Latin Grammy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Grammy) winner [Ana Tijoux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ana_Tijoux).[^cite_note-736]

On 15 February 2024, protests were held in [Providencia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providencia,_Chile), [Santiago province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Province,_Chile), demanding an end to Israeli aggression on Gaza and the severing of [relations with Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile%E2%80%93Israel_relations).[^cite_note-737] In March 2024, protesters called on president [Gabriel Boric](47329497.md) to expel the Israeli Ambassador from Chile.[^cite_note-738]

### Colombia



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Plaza de Bolívar, Bogotá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_Bol%C3%ADvar,_Bogot%C3%A1), Colombia, 5 July 2024

A protest was staged at the Israeli embassy in [Bogotá](211271.md) in support of Palestine, during which protesters burned the Israeli flag.[^cite_note-739]

### Guyana

On 21 October 2023, hundreds of Guyanese marched through [Georgetown](56702.md) to protest Israel's war on Gaza and chanted "From the river to the sea, Palestine will be free... Free Palestine! Free Palestine!"[^cite_note-740] In May 2024, a pro-Palestine rally was held at the Square of the Revolution in Georgetown, denouncing Israel's violence towards Palestinians.[^cite_note-741]

### Suriname

In November 2023, hundreds of protesters, including Muslims and Jews, peacefully marched through [Paramaribo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramaribo) to protest Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-742]

### Uruguay

On 10 October, around a thousand people demonstrated in support of Israel in [Punta del Este](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punta_del_Este).[^cite_note-743] It was attended by the Intendant of [Maldonado Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maldonado_Department) [Enrique Antía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Ant%C3%ADa) and representatives of other religions.[^cite_note-744] Additionally, [*La Mano*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Mano_de_Punta_del_Este) was lit up in the colors of the Israeli flag.[^cite_note-745] On the night of 11 October, a demonstration was held in [Montevideo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montevideo) in support of Israel, which brought together around 3,000 people on the [Rambla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rambla_of_Montevideo) in the [Pocitos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pocitos) neighborhood.[^cite_note-746][^cite_note-747] The rally, which was called by different organizations of the [Jewish community of Uruguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Uruguay) "against terrorism" was attended by a number of government officials, including Vice President [Beatriz Argimón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatriz_Argim%C3%B3n).[^cite_note-748][^cite_note-749]

### Venezuela

In [Caracas](55289.md), protestors rallied to show solidarity with Palestine.[^cite_note-aljazeera-207]

## Worldwide

### Advocacy groups

On 13 November 2023, 30 organizations, including [Amnesty International](18947898.md), the [Evangelical Lutheran Church](248255.md), and [Oxfam](19698439.md), signed an open letter to the [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration), urging them not to transfer [155mm artillery shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M110_155_mm_projectile) to Israel.[^cite_note-Aid,_advocacy_groups-750]

On 2 February 2024, more than 800 serving officials from the United States and Europe released the "trans-Atlantic statement" opposing their governments' support of Israel's war in Gaza, saying the US and EU risked contributing "to grave violations of international law, war crimes and even ethnic cleansing or genocide".[^cite_note-751]

#### Amnesty International

Amnesty International condemned European authorities for enforcing limitations on protests, contending that these actions infringe upon freedom of speech. It stated that nonviolent demonstrations against Israel should not be treated as a matter of security, adding that prohibiting pro-Palestinian protests, intimidating or arresting individuals who voice their opinions on the issue, and threatening to expel foreign nationals involved in such actions violate human rights.[^cite_note-752]

### Artists

See also: [Blockout 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blockout_2024)

Artists around the world expressed vocal support for Israel and Palestine.[^cite_note-753]

#### Pro-Palestine and anti-war

Further information: [Artists4Ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artists4Ceasefire)

On 21 October, a group of celebrities wrote an [open letter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_letter) to US President Biden calling for an immediate ceasefire.[^cite_note-754] Actors expressing support for Palestine faced repercussions for perceived antisemitism, including [Melissa Barrera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melissa_Barrera) and [Susan Sarandon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_Sarandon). Barrera had compared Gaza to a [concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration_camp), while Sarandon had remarked at a rally: "There are a lot of people afraid of being Jewish at this time, and are getting a taste of what it feels like to be a Muslim in this country."[^cite_note-755][^cite_note-756] She later apologized, calling her phrasing "a terrible mistake".[^cite_note-757] Artists across [Europe](9239.md) faced repercussions for statements in solidarity with Palestinians.[^cite_note-758] Painter [Ai Weiwei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ai_Weiwei)'s exhibition at the [Lisson Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisson_Gallery) was postponed indefinitely after he tweeted about the conflict that "the sense of guilt around the persecution of the Jewish people has been, at times, transferred to offset the Arab world. Financially, culturally, and in terms of media influence, the Jewish community has had a significant presence in the United States".[^cite_note-759][^cite_note-760] Gallerists reported collectors were requesting artists critical of Israel be removed from shows.[^cite_note-761] [Adam Broomberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Broomberg), a Jewish artist from South Africa, faced cancellations and accusations of antisemitism for his support of Palestine.[^cite_note-762] British musician [Roger Waters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Waters) was reportedly dropped by [BMG Rights Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMG_Rights_Management) for his anti-Israel statements.[^cite_note-763]



A demonstration against Israeli [Eurovision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2024) participation in Barcelona, April 2024

In an interview with [Democracy Now!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_Now!), author [Ta-Nehisi Coates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta-Nehisi_Coates) stated "the most shocking thing" about the conflict was that it was not complicated, comparing his experience as an [African American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_American) to that of the Palestinians.[^cite_note-764] An open-letter by 350 authors accused the [Frankfurt Book Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt_Book_Fair) of "shutting down" Palestinian voices after it postponed the awarding of its annual prize to Palestinian author [Adania Shibli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adania_Shibli).[^cite_note-765] The photographer [Nan Goldin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nan_Goldin) cancelled a project with the *[New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Times)* due to its complicity with Israel.[^cite_note-766] Tunisian actress [Hend Sabri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hend_Sabri) stepped down as a [World Food Programme](33582.md) [Goodwill ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodwill_ambassador) over [Israel's food blockade in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_blockade_of_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-767] [Angelina Jolie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelina_Jolie) condemned Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-768] [Lawrence Abu Hamdan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_Abu_Hamdan), [Catherine David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_David), and [Judith Butler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Butler) signed an open-letter to start an artistic and academic boycott of German institutions.[^cite_note-769] [Ken Loach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Loach) carried a sign reading "Gaza – Stop the massacre" at the [BAFTAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/77th_British_Academy_Film_Awards).[^cite_note-770]

A group of 1,300 artists, including actors [Olivia Colman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivia_Colman), [Harriet Walter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harriet_Walter), and [Juliet Stevenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliet_Stevenson) called for a ceasefire and condemned western cultural institutions for "repressing, silencing and stigmatising Palestinian voices and perspectives".[^cite_note-771] American director [Michael Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Moore) vocally advocated against the "slaughter in Gaza".[^cite_note-772] On 13 December, the Icelandic Society of Authors and Composers urged [RÚV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%9AV) to withdraw from the [Eurovision Song Contest 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2024) unless Israel was barred from participating.[^cite_note-773] More than 1000 artists vowed to cease working with the British gallery [Arnolfini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnolfini,_Bristol) following its decision to cancel two Palestinian film events.[^cite_note-774] On 13 December, the [Heinrich Böll Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinrich_B%C3%B6ll_Foundation) withdrew from the [Hannah Arendt Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannah_Arendt_Prize) ceremony for [Masha Gessen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masha_Gessen), due to their *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker)* article comparing Gazans to ghettoized European Jews.[^cite_note-775] [Olly Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olly_Alexander) faced controversy when he signed a petition criticizing [Israeli apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_apartheid) and the [Palestinian genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation).[^cite_note-776]

During an acceptance speech, novelist [Arundhati Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arundhati_Roy) said: "If we say nothing about Israel's brazen slaughter of Palestinians, even as it is livestreamed into the most private recesses of our personal lives, we are complicit in it."[^cite_note-777] Artists in France, Italy, Mexico, South Africa, Germany, the United States, Belgium, and Britain, including [Caryl Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caryl_Churchill) and [Dominic Cooke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Cooke), protested in support of the arrested artists of [The Freedom Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Freedom_Theatre) in the West Bank.[^cite_note-778] Palestinian sculptor Sana Farah Bishara sculpted a bronze statue of the infant Jesus in an incubator for the [Church of the Nativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Nativity) in [Bethlehem](4312.md).[^cite_note-779] American singers [Taylor Swift](5422144.md) and [Selena Gomez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selena_Gomez) attended [Ramy Youssef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramy_Youssef)'s fundraiser for Gaza.[^cite_note-780]



Pro-Palestinian protest on [Hollywood Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Boulevard) in [Los Angeles](18110.md) against the genocide and [Hollywood](418334.md)'s role in dehumanizing Muslims, 15 November 2023

On 10 January 2024, *[ARTnews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ARTnews)* reported the [Eskenazi Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskenazi_Museum_of_Art) at [Indiana University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University) had cancelled its career retrospective of Palestinian painter [Samia Halaby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samia_Halaby) due to "safety concerns", to which she responded: "I am not a stranger to racism and sexism of the art world."[^cite_note-781] The [92nd Street Y](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/92nd_Street_Y) cancelled a talk by [Viet Thanh Nguyen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viet_Thanh_Nguyen), who had previously condemned Israeli violence against Gazans.[^cite_note-782] 1,400 Finnish artists and musicians called for Israel to be barred from performing in the 2024 Eurovision contest.[^cite_note-783] According to *[The Jewish Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jewish_Chronicle)*, the band [Coldplay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coldplay) posted the music video "[Freedom for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_for_Palestine)" to their official Facebook page.[^cite_note-784] A group of actors, including *[Game of Thrones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_of_Thrones)* stars [Lena Heady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lena_Heady) and [Charles Dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Dance), read out South Africa's ICJ lawsuit to bring awareness to the plight of Palestinians.[^cite_note-785]

On 15 January 2024, Bosnian author [Lana Bastašić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lana_Basta%C5%A1i%C4%87) stated she was severing ties with German publishing house [S. Fischer Verlag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._Fischer_Verlag) due to its failure to speak out against the "genocide" in Gaza.[^cite_note-786] Audience members reportedly walked out of American comedian [Dave Chappelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Chappelle)'s show after he criticized U.S. support for Israeli war crimes.[^cite_note-787] American comedian [Dauood Naimyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dauood_Naimyar?action=edit\&redlink=1) had shows cancelled for his pro-Palestinian social media posts.[^cite_note-788] [Emily Jacir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Jacir), a Palestinian-American filmmaker, criticized the "harassment, baseless smear campaigns, \[and] cancelling shows" of Palestinian artists.[^cite_note-789] [Randa Jarrar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randa_Jarrar), a Palestinian-American writer, was physically removed from a [PEN America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEN_America) event in Los Angeles for disrupting the event; five other people who were also protesting against the speaker, Mayim Bialik, were not removed.[^cite_note-790][^cite_note-791]

Welsh singer [Charlotte Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlotte_Church) led a choir in singing a rendition of a pro-Palestinian song titled "From the River to the Sea".[^cite_note-792] Several filmmakers at the [Berlin International Film Festival](18948039.md) criticized the war, including American director [Ben Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Russell_\(filmmaker\)) and Palestinian journalist [Basel Adra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basel_Adra).[^cite_note-793] More than 4,000 artists signed a petition calling for Israel to be excluded from the [Venice Biennale](301876.md).[^cite_note-794] American actress [Hunter Schafer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_Schafer) was arrested in New York protesting against Biden and his response to the war.[^cite_note-795] [Zukiswa Wanner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zukiswa_Wanner) and [Mohamed Abla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Abla) returned their [Goethe Medals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goethe_Medal) in protest of Germany's support of Israel.[^cite_note-796] During the [2024 Oscars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/96th_Academy_Awards) on 11 March, director [Jonathan Glazer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Glazer) condemned the "hijacking" of Judaism and the Holocaust in support of Israel's occupation of the Palestinian Territories.[^cite_note-797] Editors at *[Guernica magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernica_\(magazine\))* resigned en masse following the publication of an article termed "apologia for Zionism and the ongoing genocide in Palestine".[^cite_note-798] At least 80 scheduled performers at the [SXSW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SXSW) festival pulled out in protest of sponsors' ties to the war.[^cite_note-799] [Rio Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Cinema,_Dalston), the venue of London's largest screening of Eurovision, cancelled its 2024 event, stating that it had decided against screening the program's grand final "while Israel remains in the competition".[^cite_note-800] In May 2024, [Dave Chappelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Chappelle) stated Gaza was facing a genocide.[^cite_note-801] In October 2024, 1,000 writers and literary professionals pledged a cultural boycott of Israel.[^cite_note-802]

In May 2025, more than 300 British artists, doctors, activists and academics including [Benedict Cumberbatch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedict_Cumberbatch), [Dua Lipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dua_Lipa), [Gary Lineker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Lineker), [Danny Boyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Boyle), and [Brian Cox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Cox_\(actor\)) signed an open letter to Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) to suspend arms sales to Israel and to broker a permanent ceasefire.[^cite_note-803] In that same month, more than 400 writers including [Jeanette Winterson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeanette_Winterson), [Irvine Welsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irvine_Welsh), [Kate Mosse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Mosse), and [Zadie Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zadie_Smith) signed a letter calling for an immediate ceasefire and denounced Israel's actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-804]

#### Pro-Israel

American celebrities, including [Amy Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Schumer),[^cite_note-805] [Mayim Bialik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayim_Bialik),[^cite_note-806] [Sarah Silverman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Silverman),[^cite_note-807] and [Noah Schnapp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noah_Schnapp)[^cite_note-808] faced controversy from their fan bases for their support for Israel. A group of 700 celebrities condemned Hamas.[^cite_note-809] American actress [Jamie Lee Curtis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_Lee_Curtis) and Canadian musician [Justin Bieber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Bieber) faced controversy for supporting Israel using images from the Gaza Strip, and then deleting the posts after finding out the pictures showed Palestinians.[^cite_note-810][^cite_note-811] American film director [Quentin Tarantino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quentin_Tarantino) made a trip in mid-October to "boost IDF morale" in southern Israel.[^cite_note-812] Several hundred Hollywood figures including [Jordan Peele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Peele), [Chris Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Rock), [Taika Waititi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taika_Waititi), [James Corden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Corden), [Matthew Weiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Weiner), [Aaron Sorkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Sorkin), [Judd Apatow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judd_Apatow), [Eugene Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_Levy), [Jon Hamm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Hamm), [Will Ferrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_Ferrell), [Jeff Goldblum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Goldblum),[^cite_note-\:15-813] [Bob Odenkirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Odenkirk) and [Tiffany Haddish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiffany_Haddish) urged the release of Israeli hostages and supported US President Biden's stance in the war on 23 October.[^cite_note-814] American actor [Timothee Chalamet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timothee_Chalamet) faced backlash for an [SNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SNL) skit, with fans stating he was "mocking genocide".[^cite_note-815][^cite_note-816] American singer [Beyoncé](83688.md) was criticized for screening her 2023 concert film *[Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance:_A_Film_by_Beyonc%C3%A9)* in Israel during the war.[^cite_note-817] British comedian [Stephen Fry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Fry) was accused of "drawing more attention to himself" than the conflict during a holiday special.[^cite_note-818][^cite_note-819] In mid-December 2023, American comedian [Jerry Seinfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Seinfeld) traveled to Israel to "show solidarity" with Israelis.[^cite_note-820]

American comedian [Chelsea Handler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelsea_Handler) faced criticism for posting a pro-Israel TikTok that was described as "propaganda".[^cite_note-821] American actress [Selma Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selma_Blair) was forced to apologize after calling pro-Palestinian activists "terrorist supporting goons" and advocating for their [deportation](412990.md).[^cite_note-822] Four hundred celebrities, including British actress [Helen Mirren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helen_Mirren), British singer [Boy George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boy_George), American producer [Scooter Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scooter_Braun), and American actresses [Julianna Margulies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julianna_Margulies) and [Emmy Rossum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmy_Rossum) signed a letter supporting Israel's participation in the [2024 Eurovision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2024) competition.[^cite_note-823] In February 2024, American [Kid Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kid_Rock) advocated killing "30,000 to 40,000" Palestinian civilians at a time until Israeli hostages were returned.[^cite_note-824][^cite_note-825] [Nick Cave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Cave) and [Thom Yorke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thom_Yorke) were criticized in an [Al Jazeera English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English) editorial by [Andrew Mitrovica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Mitrovica?action=edit\&redlink=1) for their decision to continue playing concerts in Israel.[^cite_note-826] In October 2024, over 1,000 figures from the literary and entertainment industries signed a letter denouncing attempts to "boycott, harass and scapegoat Jewish and Israeli authors and literary institutions."[^cite_note-827]

### Sports

Sports stars and fans protested the conflict. The [Green Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Brigade) pledged to continue supporting Palestine despite backlash from the [Celtic F.C.](69708.md) board.[^cite_note-828] [Youcef Atal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youcef_Atal) and [Anwar El Ghazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_El_Ghazi) were both suspended for supporting Palestine.[^cite_note-829] French Interior Minister [Gérald Darmanin](54070416.md) accused [Karim Benzema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Benzema) of being connected to the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md) for his support of Palestine.[^cite_note-830] [Ons Jabeur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ons_Jabeur) donated part of her [WTA Finals](1356185.md) prize money to the Palestinian people.[^cite_note-831]

In Australia, [Usman Khawaja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usman_Khawaja) faced a ban from cricket if he had played with the messages "all lives are equal" and "freedom is a human right" in the colors of the Palestinian flag on his shoes; under cricket bylaws, "messages which relate to political, religious or racial activities or causes" are not permitted.[^cite_note-832][^cite_note-833][^cite_note-834] In the United States, the [Minnesota Vikings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Vikings) and other NFL teams wore shoes that stated "I stand with Israel".[^cite_note-835] Attendees at the [2023 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_AFC_Asian_Cup) chanted "Free Palestine" during the UAE-Palestine game on 18 January.[^cite_note-836]

More than 300 Palestinian sports leagues called for Israel to be barred from the [2024 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Olympics) after it killed Palestine's Olympic football team coach and destroyed the headquarters of the [Palestine Olympic Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Olympic_Committee).[^cite_note-837] [Gary Lineker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Lineker), a British sports commentator, shared then deleted a post calling for an international ban on Israeli sports competition.[^cite_note-838] Attendees at the [2024 NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_NBA_All-Star_Game) held banners reading "Let Gaza Live" and "Ceasefire Now".[^cite_note-839] Russian UFC fighter [Umar Nurmagomedov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umar_Nurmagomedov) dedicated his victory speech to the people in Gaza, stating "they don't have any food, they don't have any water, medical, nothing" and called on the world to "wake up".[^cite_note-840] In March 2024, the [Palestinian Football Association](5531531.md) called on [FIFA](11049.md) to issue sanctions against Israel, stating Israel had killed "no less than 99 footballers" in Gaza.[^cite_note-841]

### Boycotts

See also: [Boycott, Divestment and Sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boycott,_Divestment_and_Sanctions)



Demonstration outside [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation)'s office in [Goleta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goleta,_California), California to protest the military contractor's supply of weapons to Israel, 9 November 2023

In response to the conflict, young activists on social media began widespread [boycotts](18591996.md) against companies perceived to be supporting the Israeli military during the conflict.[^cite_note-What_does_Gen-Z_think-842] The three primary targets were [McDonald's](2480627.md), [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks) and [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+), with the hashtag #boycottstarbucks gaining 29 million views on [TikTok](56822861.md) by 3 November.[^cite_note-What_does_Gen-Z_think-842] McDonald's was targeted after offering free food for the IDF.[^cite_note-The_boycott_movement-843] Starbucks was boycotted after it sued the [Starbucks Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks_unions) for a social media post supporting the people of Gaza.[^cite_note-The_boycott_movement-843] [Zara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zara_\(retailer\)) faced boycotts after its marketing campaign featured rubble and body bags.[^cite_note-844] Protesters targeted [Zara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zara_\(retailer\)) stores in Germany, Australia, Portugal, the Netherlands, Tunisia, Turkey and Scotland.[^cite_note-845]

On 7 November, the [Grand National Assembly of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_National_Assembly_of_Turkey) announced a boycott of companies supporting Israel, barring their products from being sold in the legislature's restaurants, cafeterias, or teahouses.[^cite_note-846] In [Egypt](8087628.md), a nationwide "boycott Israel" movement resulted in the revival of local brands and products.[^cite_note-847] In Malaysia, the licensee of McDonald's sued the [BDS movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BDS_movement) for their social media posts linking the chain to Israel's "genocidal war against Palestinians in Gaza".[^cite_note-848] BDS activists in France targeted [Carrefour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrefour) supermarkets with protests for their ties to Israeli settler companies.[^cite_note-849] A large banner hung in the center of [Madrid](41188263.md) calling for a boycott of McDonald's in February 2024.[^cite_note-850] Turkish-founded water company Sirma faced boycott calls after a video showing a group of Israeli soldiers drinking their product in Gaza went viral on [X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_\(website\)).[^cite_note-851] In Turkey, boycott campaigns have targeted Starbucks, McDonald's, [Burger King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burger_King), [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola) and Carrefour.[^cite_note-852] Boycotts of McDonalds and Starbucks severely impacted the chains in Southeast Asia.[^cite_note-853]



Pro-Palestinian protest outside [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks) in [Exeter, England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exeter,_England) on 10 February 2024

On 4 January 2024, McDonald's CEO [Chris Kempczinski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Kempczinski) stated the boycotts were hurting sales across the Middle East.[^cite_note-854] On 31 January, Starbucks CEO [Laxman Narasimhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laxman_Narasimhan) stated that boycotts of the company in the US and Middle East had caused the company to lower its yearly sales forecasts.[^cite_note-855] In March 2024, Starbucks announced more than 2,000 layoffs across the Middle East and North Africa due to the boycotts.[^cite_note-856]

[Direct action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_action) was taken at [arms factories](608296.md) in the United States and the United Kingdom that supplied arms to Israel, such as [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin),[^cite_note-857][^cite_note-858] [General Dynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics),[^cite_note-859] [Textron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textron),[^cite_note-860] [Boeing](18933266.md),[^cite_note-861] [L3Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L3Harris),[^cite_note-862] [Raytheon Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation) and [Northrop Grumman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman).[^cite_note-863] For instance, on 10 November 2023, trade unionists in [Rochester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rochester,_Kent), Kent, blocked the entrances to a [BAE Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAE_Systems) factory, stating the facility manufactured [military aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_aircraft) components used to bomb Gaza;[^cite_note-864] and on 16 November, [Palestine Action](67777543.md) occupied a [Leonardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_S.p.A.) factory in [Southampton](7920751.md), stopping production.[^cite_note-SimoneDailyEcho20231116-501] [ZIM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZIM_\(shipping_company\)) was barred from entering ports in [Malaysia](3607937.md).[^cite_note-865]

Student protesters called on Columbia University to financially divest from any company with business ties to the Israeli government, including [Microsoft](19001.md), [Google](1092923.md) and [Amazon](90451.md).[^cite_note-866] NYU Alumni for Palestine called on New York University to "terminate all vendor contracts with companies playing active roles in the military occupation in Palestine and ongoing genocide in Gaza, namely [Cisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisco), Lockheed Martin, [Caterpillar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_Inc.) and [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric)".[^cite_note-867]

#### Limit request

Pro-Palestinian protesters in France demanded to limit the participation of Israeli athletes in the Olympics. These people gathered in front of the headquarters of the Olympic Games in Paris.[^cite_note-868]

### Corporations



Pro-Palestinian protest in Ottawa, Canada, 23 November 2023

American companies have expressed support for Israel, including financial support.[^cite_note-869] Yale Professor [Jeffrey Sonnenfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Sonnenfeld) made a "list of companies have spoken out to condemn Hamas's terrorist attack on Israel".[^cite_note-870] According to *[Al Jazeera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English)*, few major corporations have expressed a position on "Israel's retaliatory air raids on Gaza".[^cite_note-871] Some companies have been criticized for their support; others have been criticized for their silence.[^cite_note-872][^cite_note-873] On 16 January 2024, [Ben & Jerry's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_&_Jerry's) became one of the first multinational corporations to call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-874]

### Global protests

On 11 December 2023, a global strike was called, with activists encouraging participants to refrain from going to work, school, or making any purchases.[^cite_note-875] Supporters of the strike blocked the entrance of *[The New York Times](30680.md)*.[^cite_note-876]

On 13 January 2024, on the 100th day since the beginning of the war, protests for a ceasefire were held around the world, including in London, Paris, Vienna, Berlin, Johannesburg, Amman, Washington DC, Baghdad, and Chicago.[^cite_note-877][^cite_note-878]

Journalists of [Agence France-Presse](471981.md) staged protests around the world in support of their colleagues in Gaza, with the AFP global news director stating, "The AFP newsroom wanted to express its whole-hearted support for our Gaza colleagues who are working in dire conditions and under constant fear of bombardment".[^cite_note-879]

### Technology

Activists used social media, such as [X](9988187.md) and [TikTok](56822861.md), to share information about the war.[^cite_note-880] Pro-Palestinian activists adopted the watermelon emoji[^cite_note-881] [as a symbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watermelon_as_a_Palestinian_symbol) to represent solidarity with the people of Gaza.[^cite_note-882] [TikTok](56822861.md) was a source of ire for some, with people such as former US president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) criticizing "TikTok activism" for obscuring context.[^cite_note-883] Celebrities, including [Sacha Baron Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacha_Baron_Cohen) and [Amy Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Schumer), held a private meeting with TikTok executives accusing them of spreading antisemitism.[^cite_note-884] TikTok stated it was not biased, but that young people were organically more supportive of Palestine.[^cite_note-885] On 18 November, [Elon Musk](909036.md) announced any user who used the phrases "[decolonization](302084.md)" or "[from the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea)" would be suspended from X.[^cite_note-886][^cite_note-889] Pro-Palestinian content creators in the U.S. reported widespread [shadowbanning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shadowbanning).[^cite_note-890] Critics of Israel also alleged they were censored or shadowbanned on the comment sections of [Finnish national media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Finland) outlets in November.[^cite_note-891]

*[Anonymous Sudan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anonymous_Sudan)*, a hacker group, launched a [DDoS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distributed_Denial_of_Service) attack on [ChatGPT](72417803.md)[^cite_note-892][^cite_note-aakash-893] after Tal Broda – a member of [OpenAI](48795986.md)'s leadership – made social media posts which expressed support for Israel and called for more intense bombing in Gaza.[^cite_note-aakash-893][^cite_note-894][^cite_note-895]

In January 2024, the Israeli government reportedly purchased a technological system for conducting large-scale influence campaigns online.[^cite_note-896] In February 2024, Israeli supporters adopted AI tools to report pro-Palestinian content en masse for supposedly violating site guidelines.[^cite_note-897]

[Misinformation in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misinformation_in_the_Gaza_war) is generated by [machine learning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_learning) models were used by activists to solicit support, as well as to create the artificial impression of broader support.[^cite_note-898] AI-generated images and [deepfakes](56641018.md) went viral online, though they were simultaneously fact-checked.[^cite_note-899][^cite_note-900] Technology companies were accused of profiting from AI-generated images related to the war,[^cite_note-901] as well as for building models that generated content reflecting [anti-Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Palestinian_racism) biases.[^cite_note-902]

The [Israeli prosecutor's office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Attorney_\(Israel\)) sent over 8,000 removal requests to [Meta](62420226.md) and TikTok for content related to the war, resulting in 94% of the requests being removed.[^cite_note-903] *[The Intercept](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Intercept)* reported Meta had allowed Hebrew and Arabic-language ads, generated by digital rights advocates to test the limits of [Facebook](7529378.md)'s machine-learning moderation, calling for a "holocaust for the Palestinians".[^cite_note-904] In December 2023, Meta's independent [oversight board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oversight_Board_\(Meta\)) released a report stating Facebook overly censored conflict-related posts, with civil rights organizations stating Meta had suppressed Palestinian content.[^cite_note-905]

### Trade unions

On 16 October, the [Palestinian General Federation of Trade Unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_General_Federation_of_Trade_Unions) issued an urgent call for international trade unions to halt the arms trade to Israel.[^cite_note-906] On 31 October, transport workers' unions in [Belgium](3343.md) instructed their members to refuse to handle military equipment headed to Israel.[^cite_note-907] On 7 November, the port stevedores' union in [Barcelona](4443.md) refused to handle military material citing the war on Gaza, although the Spanish government said it had no plans to supply lethal weaponry for the conflict.[^cite_note-908] On 9 November, more than 2,900 doctors in the [United Kingdom](31717.md) signed an open letter to the [British Medical Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Medical_Association) demanding action to support Gaza.[^cite_note-909] On 13 November, ten major trade unions in [India](14533.md) called the war on Gaza "shamelessly genocidal" and urged workers to refuse to handle Israeli cargo.[^cite_note-910] On 18 February, the Water Transport Workers Federation of India stated it would not load or unload weapons to Israel.[^cite_note-911]

### Universities















**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University_pro-Palestinian_campus_protests_and_occupations_during_the_Gaza_war)

- [Ohio State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University)

- [Monash University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monash_University)

- [University of Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_University_of_Amsterdam_pro-Palestinian_campus_occupations)

- [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University)

- [University of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sydney)

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Gaza war protests at universities § Protests on campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_universities#Protests_on_campuses).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gaza_war_protests_at_universities\&action=edit)]

By May 6, 2024, [student protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Student_protest) had occurred in 45 of the 50 states [in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses_in_the_United_States_in_2024) and the District of Columbia, with encampments, [occupations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_\(protest\)), [walkouts](2016860.md) or [sit-ins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in) on almost 140 campuses.[^cite_note-912] Thirty-four encampments were established in the United Kingdom;[^cite_note-913] across universities in Australia,[^cite_note-914] beginning with the [University of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sydney);[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Dumas-2024-915] and in Canada, including an [encampment at McGill University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McGill_University_pro-Palestinian_encampment).[^cite_note-916][^cite_note-917] On May 7, protests spread further on European campuses after mass arrests at the [University of Amsterdam campus occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_University_of_Amsterdam_pro-Palestinian_campus_occupations),[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_AP_News-2024a-918] including occupation of campus buildings at [Leipzig University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leipzig_University) in Germany, [Sciences Po](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Sciences_Po_pro-Palestinian_occupation_protest) in France, and [Ghent University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghent_University) in Belgium.[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Kassam-2024-919] By May 8, protests had taken place in more than 25 countries,[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Al_Jazeera-2024c2-920] and on May 13, approximately 1,000 Dutch students and university staff took part in a national [walk-out](2016860.md).[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Sky_News-921]

### Queer Groups

This section is an excerpt from [LGBTQ advocacy in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war\&action=edit)]

[LGBTQ advocacy in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war) has remained a significantly contentious topic. Many [LGBTQ people](78174325.md) allied themselves with pro-Palestinian causes, especially in protest movements against the ongoing war, and against the [Gaza genocide](75668427.md).[^cite_note-LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war_Prager-2024-922][^cite_note-LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war_\:2-923] Some pro-Palestinian LGBTQ people have claimed common cause between [queer liberation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queer_liberation) and [Palestinian liberation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_liberation),[^cite_note-LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war_NBC_News-2024-924] though others have called such movements [antisemitic](1078.md)[^cite_note-LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war_\:4-925] and self-contradictory. Some pro-Israeli LGBTQ people have complained about feeling excluded from traditionally LGBTQ spaces due to pro-Palestinian LGBTQ advocacy.[^cite_note-LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war_\:4-925][^cite_note-926]

### 13 October

[Khaled Meshaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_Meshaal), a former leader of Hamas, appealed on 6 October for people "head to the squares and streets" in solidarity with Gaza on 13 October. In a later statement on 10 October, Meshaal explicitly called for protests in all cities with Muslim communities.[^cite_note-checknews-73][^cite_note-927][^cite_note-928] As a result of [distorted rumours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmission_chain_method), media groups reported he had called for "global day of jihad" or a "day of rage", though his reported comments included neither phrase.[^cite_note-dayofjihad-929][^cite_note-dayofrage-930] While the word "[jihad](16203.md)" can refer to non-violent struggle, it has often been used radical groups to refer more narrowly to a [holy war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_war), and some took Meshaal's comments to advocate violence.[^cite_note-snopes-72][^cite_note-931]

The [Israeli National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_National_Security_Council) and the [Foreign Ministry](18133296.md) issued a press release cautioning protests were "liable to turn violent".[^cite_note-jointstate-932][^cite_note-checknews-73] The Israeli government advised its citizens overseas to avoid any demonstrations or protests on 13 October.[^cite_note-933][^cite_note-934][^cite_note-jointstate-932] Rumours and fears were widespread, and "day of jihad"-related threats were made against Jews and Muslims.[^cite_note-dayofrage-930][^cite_note-dayofjihad-929] There was a heightened police presence at Jewish and Muslim sites, events were cancelled, and children stayed home from Jewish schools.[^cite_note-935][^cite_note-\:0-172]

As a result of the reports, rallies opposing Israeli military action were banned in France, and restricted in Germany and Hungary.[^cite_note-snopes-72][^cite_note-checknews-73][^cite_note-WP13Oct-303][^cite_note-ABC-936][^cite_note-thousands13october-937][^cite_note-938] Germany's ban specifically barred praise for murder or manslaughter, burning of the [Israeli flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_flag), or praise for the Hamas attack or the use of its symbols.[^cite_note-protesters13october-546] Peaceful protests occurred around the world on 13 October.[^cite_note-thousands13october-937][^cite_note-protesters13october-546]  There were no major incidents in the [Al-Aqsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Aqsa) area.[^cite_note-\:0-172] In Jordan, police fired tear gas at about 500 protestors approaching the border with the West Bank. Anti-Israel protests in the border areas were banned, but licensed protests were permitted elsewhere.[^cite_note-JordanDispersesProtestersReuters-549] In the West Bank, four Palestinian protestors were killed by Israeli forces. Seven had been killed the previous day.[^cite_note-939]

An [Ingush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingush_people) man carried out [a stabbing attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arras_school_stabbing) at a school in [Arras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arras), France, killing a teacher and injuring three staff members, in an attack that was linked to the supposed "Global Day of Jihad".[^cite_note-snopes-72] The suspect was previously known to the French security services for his involvement with [radical Islamism](8734632.md).[^cite_note-940][^cite_note-941]

On 14 October, [a six-year-old boy in Illinois named Wadea al-Fayoume was killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Wadea_al-Fayoume), by his 71-year-old landlord, who was said by his wife to have been afraid of Hamas' "national day of jihad" and convinced something would happen.[^cite_note-SunTimesOct16-942][^cite_note-snopes-72][^cite_note-checknews-73]

## Responses

Further information: [Impacts of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impacts_of_the_Gaza_war) and [Reactions to the 2024 pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses)

See also: [Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_during_the_Gaza_war), [Anti-Palestinianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Palestinian_racism_during_the_Gaza_war), and [Islamophobia during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobia_during_the_Gaza_war)



Pro-Israel protest in London, 16 October

On 17 October, a group of 75 Israeli politicians and [public intellectuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_intellectuals) identified with [left-wing politics](18499.md) published an open letter addressing what they called "a disturbing trend in the global Left's political culture".[^cite_note-Newsweek_2023-10-18-943][^cite_note-El_País_2023-10-17-944] The authors, which include [Aviad Kleinberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aviad_Kleinberg), [Ibtisam Mara'ana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibtisam_Mara'ana), [Eva Illouz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eva_Illouz), [Ortal Ben Dayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ortal_Ben_Dayan), [Orna Ben-Naftali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orna_Ben-Naftali), [Galia Sabar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galia_Sabar), [Dov Khenin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dov_Khenin), [David Grossman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Grossman), [Taleb el-Sana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taleb_el-Sana), [Mossi Raz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mossi_Raz), [Amit Schejter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amit_Schejter) and [Ruth Halperin-Kaddari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Halperin-Kaddari), acknowledged their many peers in the Arab world and elsewhere that have condemned the attack, but noted with dismay that "some elements within the global Left... have reacted with indifference to these horrific events and sometimes even justified Hamas's actions". They state that "\[Hamas's] [core commitments](13913.md#Hamas_Charter) are fundamentally inconsistent with [progressive principles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_politics)", and emphasize that "there is no contradiction between staunchly opposing the Israeli subjugation and occupation of Palestinians and unequivocally condemning brutal acts of violence against innocent civilians. In fact, every consistent Leftist must hold both positions simultaneously.[^cite_note-Newsweek_2023-10-18-943][^cite_note-El_País_2023-10-17-944] [Anat Kamm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anat_Kamm), [Helen Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helen_Lewis_\(journalist\)), [Yascha Mounk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yascha_Mounk), [Jonathan Chait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Chait), [Anshel Pfeffer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anshel_Pfeffer), [David Witzthum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Witzthum), [Nitzan Horowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitzan_Horowitz) and others have also observed discrepancies in the international reactions to [Hamas's attack](74998317.md) and [Israel's response](74998519.md).[^cite_note-945]

In a speech to the [United States Senate](24909346.md), [Majority Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority_Leader_of_the_U.S._Senate) [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) asked if "anybody \[can] imagine a horrific terrorist attack in another country receiving such a reception", and warned that "Antisemites are taking advantage of the pro-Palestinian movement to espouse hatred and bigotry toward Jewish people".[^cite_note-946]

[Nobel Prize](21201.md) laureate [Aaron Ciechanover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Ciechanover) criticized the presidents of "[elite American universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_League)" for their "feeble" response to the events on 7 October, for demonstrating "ignorance of the historical and geopolitical background of the [conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab-Israeli_conflict)", for allowing demonstrations "accompanied by a wave of [antisemitism](1078.md) and [anti-Israelism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Israelism)", and for failing to support Jewish students who find themselves afraid of visiting campus.[^cite_note-947]

Over 300 faculty members at the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania) signed an open letter "calling on the University community to condemn the Hamas attacks on Israel and support Israel's right to defend itself".[^cite_note-948]



Pro-Palestinian protest outside [Sproul Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sproul_Plaza) at the [University of California, Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley), 25 October

Over 300 faculty members at [University of California, Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley) signed an open letter calling for the immediate release of all hostages held by Hamas, and expressing their "disappointment" with "some student organizations' support of Hamas".[^cite_note-949]

Students at [Yale](34273.md) and [Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) universities, and the parent of a [Cornell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_University) alumnus, started petitions for the dismissal of faculty members they accused of supporting or endorsing Hamas's attack. The petitions have drawn a total of over 100,000 signatories.[^cite_note-950][^cite_note-951][^cite_note-952]

According to *[Vox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vox_\(website\))*, antisemitic incidents are on the rise in France, Germany, and the UK.[^cite_note-953]

On 28 January 2024, Congresswoman [Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi) suggested that some pro-Palestinian protesters calling for a ceasefire could be connected to [Russia](25391.md) and called on the [FBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBI) to investigate the possible connection.[^cite_note-954]

### Ban

#### Algeria

In August 2025, Algerian authorities banned a planned pro-Palestine solidarity march, citing concerns over potential political unrest. Although the government has consistently expressed support for the Palestinian cause in international forums, it has imposed restrictions on public demonstrations, including those related to Gaza. The move drew criticism from opposition figures and civil society groups, who argued that the ban contradicted Algeria's official stance and reflected broader efforts to suppress public assembly since the [2019 Hirak protests](60179677.md).[^cite_note-955]

#### Austria

In [Vienna](55866.md), police banned a pro-Palestine protest on the grounds that it used the phrase "[from the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea)" in its invites, which police alleged was a call to violence against Israelis.[^cite_note-956]

#### Fiji

Police denied an application by students of the [University of the South Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_South_Pacific) to march in solidarity with Palestine. Minister for Home Affairs [Pio Tikoduadua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pio_Tikoduadua) said that for the procession to be allowed, "they must, in fairness, grant permission to pro-Israeli groups who may wish to express their perspectives and grievances".[^cite_note-957]

#### France

France implemented a ban on all pro-Palestinian demonstrations.[^cite_note-958][^cite_note-959] Interior Minister [Gerald Darmanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerald_Darmanin) declared the ban, citing concerns related to public order. He emphasized that "Pro-Palestinian demonstrations must be prohibited because they are likely to generate disturbances to public order" and warned that organizing such protests would result in arrests.[^cite_note-960] Nonetheless, France's highest administrative court stated that pro-Palestinian protests should be assessed on a case-by-case basis, while still affirming the validity of Darmanin's directive.[^cite_note-961]

#### Germany

In [Germany](11867.md), authorities imposed prohibitions on most protests advocating for Palestinian rights[^cite_note-europe20october-962] in response to reported [anti-Semitic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Semitic) occurrences during an anti-Israel protest.[^cite_note-DWVideo20231013-319] A Berlin court upheld the ban on a scheduled pro-Palestinian demonstration in the city.[^cite_note-963] In the face of these restrictions, human rights organizations urged authorities to ensure and enable the rights of all people to express their opinions and peacefully assemble.[^cite_note-europe20october-962][^cite_note-964]

#### Hungary

Referring to planned pro-Palestine rallies in Hungary, Prime minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) said "it is shocking that there were sympathy rallies supporting the terrorists across Europe", and that Hungary "will not allow sympathy rallies supporting terrorist organisations", referring to Hamas.[^cite_note-965]

#### Singapore

The [Singapore Police Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore_Police_Force) and the [National Parks Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Parks_Board) announced that no public events and assemblies related to the war, including those in the [Speakers' Corner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speakers'_Corner,_Singapore), would be permitted.[^cite_note-966][^cite_note-967]

#### Turkey

In February 2025, Turkish authorities rejected permission for the [Republican People's Party](404748.md) (CHP) to hold a pro-Palestine march on [Galata Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galata_Bridge). The party accused the authorities of double standards and the CHP chairman of Istanbul, [ Özgür Çelik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%96zg%C3%BCr_%C3%87elik?action=edit\&redlink=1), condemned the decision.[^cite_note-968]

#### United Kingdom



Pro-Palestinian rally in London, 28 October



Pro-Palestine demonstration in [Edinburgh](9602.md), 2 December

[UK Home Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Home_Secretary) [Suella Braverman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suella_Braverman) urged police to consider the use of [Palestinian flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Palestine) and some associated chants or symbols with the Palestinian cause (such as "[from the river to the sea, Palestine will be free](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea,_Palestine_will_be_free)") in certain circumstances as a "racially aggravated" offence that should face legal consequences under [Section 5 of the Public Order Act 1986](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Section_5_of_the_Public_Order_Act_1986).[^cite_note-969] Braverman was also criticized after she accused police of applying "double standards" and favouritism in their response to demonstrations, saying that aggressive right-wing protesters were "rightly met with a stern response", while "pro-Palestinian mobs" were "largely ignored".[^cite_note-970]

Exiled Egyptian television presenter [Moataz Matar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moataz_Matar) had his UK visa cancelled by the Home Office and was placed on a watchlist barring him from reentry after he was accused of supporting Hamas following his participation in a pro-Palestine protest in London.[^cite_note-971]

British author and political commentator [Douglas Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Murray_\(author\)) claimed that pro-Palestinian protesters were openly supporting Hamas on the streets of Britain.[^cite_note-972]

#### United States

On 6 November, [Brandeis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandeis_University) became the first private university in the United States to ban its student chapter of [Students for Justice in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Justice_in_Palestine).[^cite_note-973] On 10 November, [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) suspended its campus chapters of Students for Justice in Palestine and [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace), thus barring them from hosting events on campus.[^cite_note-974] Columbia's decision was criticized by [Naomi Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Klein), [Noura Erakat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noura_Erakat), and [Sarah Schulman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Schulman), who wrote: "We have reached a new moment in American history."[^cite_note-975][^cite_note-976] After [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) banned the slogan "[from the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea)", more than 100 faculty signed an open letter to university president [Claudine Gay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claudine_Gay) criticizing her censure.[^cite_note-977]

### Praise of pro-Palestinian protests

Iran's Supreme Leader [Ayatollah Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayatollah_Ali_Khamenei) has praised the pro-Palestinian campus protests on Twitter, praising American students and suggesting they were "on the right side of history". He further described the protesting students as part of the "[resistance front](36658251.md)" against Israel and encouraged them to "become familiar with the [Quran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran)."[^cite_note-978][^cite_note-979][^cite_note-980]
